1→"""
     2→HermesAgentLoop -- Reusable Multi-Turn Agent Engine
     3→
     4→Runs the hermes-agent tool-calling loop using standard OpenAI-spec tool calling.
     5→Works with any server that returns ChatCompletion objects with tool_calls:
     6→    - Phase 1: OpenAI server type (VLLM, SGLang, OpenRouter, OpenAI API)
     7→    - Phase 2: ManagedServer with client-side tool call parser
     8→
     9→The loop passes tools= and checks response.choices[0].message.tool_calls,
    10→identical to hermes-agent's run_agent.py. Tool execution is dispatched via
    11→handle_function_call() from model_tools.py.
    12→"""
    13→
    14→import asyncio
    15→import concurrent.futures
    16→import json
    17→import logging
    18→import os
    19→import uuid
    20→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
    21→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Set
    22→
    23→from model_tools import handle_function_call
    24→
    25→# Thread pool for running sync tool calls that internally use asyncio.run()
    26→# (e.g., mini-swe-agent's modal/docker/daytona backends). Running them in a separate
    27→# thread gives them a clean event loop so they don't deadlock inside Atropos's loop.
    28→# Size must be large enough for concurrent eval tasks (e.g., 89 TB2 tasks all
    29→# making tool calls). Too small = thread pool starvation, tasks queue for minutes.
    30→# Resized at runtime by HermesAgentBaseEnv.__init__ via resize_tool_pool().
    31→_tool_executor = concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=128)
    32→
    33→
    34→def resize_tool_pool(max_workers: int):
    35→    """
    36→    Replace the global tool executor with a new one of the given size.
    37→
    38→    Called by HermesAgentBaseEnv.__init__ based on config.tool_pool_size.
    39→    Safe to call before any tasks are submitted.
    40→    """
    41→    global _tool_executor
    42→    _tool_executor = concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers)
    43→    logger.info("Tool thread pool resized to %d workers", max_workers)
    44→
    45→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    46→
    47→
    48→@dataclass
    49→class ToolError:
    50→    """Record of a tool execution error during the agent loop."""
    51→
    52→    turn: int                  # Which turn the error occurred on
    53→    tool_name: str             # Which tool was called
    54→    arguments: str             # The arguments passed (truncated)
    55→    error: str                 # The error message
    56→    tool_result: str           # The raw result returned to the model
    57→
    58→
    59→@dataclass
    60→class AgentResult:
    61→    """Result of running the agent loop."""
    62→
    63→    # Full conversation history in OpenAI message format
    64→    messages: List[Dict[str, Any]]
    65→    # ManagedServer.get_state() if available (Phase 2), None otherwise
    66→    managed_state: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None
    67→    # How many LLM calls were made
    68→    turns_used: int = 0
    69→    # True if model stopped calling tools naturally (vs hitting max_turns)
    70→    finished_naturally: bool = False
    71→    # Extracted reasoning content per turn (from PR #297 helpers)
    72→    reasoning_per_turn: List[Optional[str]] = field(default_factory=list)
    73→    # Tool errors encountered during the loop
    74→    tool_errors: List[ToolError] = field(default_factory=list)
    75→
    76→
    77→def _extract_reasoning_from_message(message) -> Optional[str]:
    78→    """
    79→    Extract reasoning content from a ChatCompletion message.
    80→
    81→    Handles multiple provider formats:
    82→    1. message.reasoning_content field (some providers)
    83→    2. message.reasoning field (some providers)
    84→    3. message.reasoning_details[].text (OpenRouter style)
    85→
    86→    Note: <think> block extraction from content is NOT done here -- that's
    87→    handled by the response already in Phase 1 (server does it) or by
    88→    ManagedServer's patch in Phase 2.
    89→
    90→    Args:
    91→        message: The assistant message from ChatCompletion response
    92→
    93→    Returns:
    94→        Extracted reasoning text, or None if not found
    95→    """
    96→    # Check reasoning_content field (common across providers)
    97→    if hasattr(message, "reasoning_content") and message.reasoning_content:
    98→        return message.reasoning_content
    99→
   100→    # Check reasoning field
   101→    if hasattr(message, "reasoning") and message.reasoning:
   102→        return message.reasoning
   103→
   104→    # Check reasoning_details (OpenRouter style)
   105→    if hasattr(message, "reasoning_details") and message.reasoning_details:
   106→        for detail in message.reasoning_details:
   107→            if hasattr(detail, "text") and detail.text:
   108→                return detail.text
   109→            if isinstance(detail, dict) and detail.get("text"):
   110→                return detail["text"]
   111→
   112→    return None
   113→
   114→
   115→class HermesAgentLoop:
   116→    """
   117→    Runs hermes-agent's tool-calling loop using standard OpenAI-spec tool calling.
   118→
   119→    Same pattern as run_agent.py:
   120→    - Pass tools= to the API
   121→    - Check response.choices[0].message.tool_calls
   122→    - Dispatch via handle_function_call()
   123→
   124→    Works identically with any server type -- OpenAI, VLLM, SGLang, OpenRouter,
   125→    or ManagedServer with a parser. The server determines how tool_calls get
   126→    populated on the response.
   127→    """
   128→
   129→    def __init__(
   130→        self,
   131→        server,
   132→        tool_schemas: List[Dict[str, Any]],
   133→        valid_tool_names: Set[str],
   134→        max_turns: int = 30,
   135→        task_id: Optional[str] = None,
   136→        temperature: float = 1.0,
   137→        max_tokens: Optional[int] = None,
   138→        extra_body: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None,
   139→    ):
   140→        """
   141→        Initialize the agent loop.
   142→
   143→        Args:
   144→            server: Server object with chat_completion() method (OpenAIServer,
   145→                    ManagedServer, ServerManager, etc.)
   146→            tool_schemas: OpenAI-format tool definitions from get_tool_definitions()
   147→            valid_tool_names: Set of tool names the model is allowed to call
   148→            max_turns: Maximum number of LLM calls before stopping
   149→            task_id: Unique ID for terminal/browser session isolation
   150→            temperature: Sampling temperature for generation
   151→            max_tokens: Max tokens per generation (None for server default)
   152→            extra_body: Extra parameters passed to the OpenAI client's create() call.
   153→                        Used for OpenRouter provider preferences, transforms, etc.
   154→                        e.g. {"provider": {"ignore": ["DeepInfra"]}}
   155→        """
   156→        self.server = server
   157→        self.tool_schemas = tool_schemas
   158→        self.valid_tool_names = valid_tool_names
   159→        self.max_turns = max_turns
   160→        self.task_id = task_id or str(uuid.uuid4())
   161→        self.temperature = temperature
   162→        self.max_tokens = max_tokens
   163→        self.extra_body = extra_body
   164→
   165→    async def run(self, messages: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> AgentResult:
   166→        """
   167→        Execute the full agent loop using standard OpenAI tool calling.
   168→
   169→        Args:
   170→            messages: Initial conversation messages (system + user).
   171→                      Modified in-place as the conversation progresses.
   172→
   173→        Returns:
   174→            AgentResult with full conversation history, managed state, and metadata
   175→        """
   176→        reasoning_per_turn = []
   177→        tool_errors: List[ToolError] = []
   178→
   179→        # Per-loop TodoStore for the todo tool (ephemeral, dies with the loop)
   180→        from tools.todo_tool import TodoStore, todo_tool as _todo_tool
   181→        _todo_store = TodoStore()
   182→
   183→        # Extract user task from first user message for browser_snapshot context
   184→        _user_task = None
   185→        for msg in messages:
   186→            if msg.get("role") == "user":
   187→                content = msg.get("content", "")
   188→                if isinstance(content, str) and content.strip():
   189→                    _user_task = content.strip()[:500]  # Cap to avoid huge strings
   190→                break
   191→
   192→        import time as _time
   193→
   194→        for turn in range(self.max_turns):
   195→            turn_start = _time.monotonic()
   196→
   197→            # Build the chat_completion kwargs
   198→            chat_kwargs = {
   199→                "messages": messages,
   200→                "n": 1,
   201→                "temperature": self.temperature,
   202→            }
   203→
   204→            # Only pass tools if we have them
   205→            if self.tool_schemas:
   206→                chat_kwargs["tools"] = self.tool_schemas
   207→
   208→            # Only pass max_tokens if explicitly set
   209→            if self.max_tokens is not None:
   210→                chat_kwargs["max_tokens"] = self.max_tokens
   211→
   212→            # Inject extra_body for provider-specific params (e.g., OpenRouter
   213→            # provider preferences like banned/preferred providers, transforms)
   214→            if self.extra_body:
   215→                chat_kwargs["extra_body"] = self.extra_body
   216→
   217→            # Make the API call -- standard OpenAI spec
   218→            api_start = _time.monotonic()
   219→            try:
   220→                response = await self.server.chat_completion(**chat_kwargs)
   221→            except Exception as e:
   222→                api_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - api_start
   223→                logger.error("API call failed on turn %d (%.1fs): %s", turn + 1, api_elapsed, e)
   224→                return AgentResult(
   225→                    messages=messages,
   226→                    managed_state=self._get_managed_state(),
   227→                    turns_used=turn + 1,
   228→                    finished_naturally=False,
   229→                    reasoning_per_turn=reasoning_per_turn,
   230→                    tool_errors=tool_errors,
   231→                )
   232→
   233→            api_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - api_start
   234→
   235→            if not response or not response.choices:
   236→                logger.warning("Empty response on turn %d (api=%.1fs)", turn + 1, api_elapsed)
   237→                return AgentResult(
   238→                    messages=messages,
   239→                    managed_state=self._get_managed_state(),
   240→                    turns_used=turn + 1,
   241→                    finished_naturally=False,
   242→                    reasoning_per_turn=reasoning_per_turn,
   243→                    tool_errors=tool_errors,
   244→                )
   245→
   246→            assistant_msg = response.choices[0].message
   247→
   248→            # Extract reasoning content from the response (all provider formats)
   249→            reasoning = _extract_reasoning_from_message(assistant_msg)
   250→            reasoning_per_turn.append(reasoning)
   251→
   252→            # Check for tool calls -- standard OpenAI spec.
   253→            # Fallback: if response has no structured tool_calls but content
   254→            # contains raw tool call tags (e.g. <tool_call>), parse them using
   255→            # hermes-agent's standalone parsers. This handles the case where
   256→            # ManagedServer's ToolCallTranslator couldn't parse because vLLM
   257→            # isn't installed.
   258→            if (
   259→                not assistant_msg.tool_calls
   260→                and assistant_msg.content
   261→                and self.tool_schemas
   262→                and "<tool_call>" in (assistant_msg.content or "")
   263→            ):
   264→                try:
   265→                    from environments.tool_call_parsers import get_parser
   266→                    fallback_parser = get_parser("hermes")
   267→                    parsed_content, parsed_calls = fallback_parser.parse(
   268→                        assistant_msg.content
   269→                    )
   270→                    if parsed_calls:
   271→                        assistant_msg.tool_calls = parsed_calls
   272→                        if parsed_content is not None:
   273→                            assistant_msg.content = parsed_content
   274→                        logger.debug(
   275→                            "Fallback parser extracted %d tool calls from raw content",
   276→                            len(parsed_calls),
   277→                        )
   278→                except Exception:
   279→                    pass  # Fall through to no tool calls
   280→
   281→            if assistant_msg.tool_calls:
   282→                # Normalize tool calls to dicts — they may come as objects
   283→                # (OpenAI API) or dicts (vLLM ToolCallTranslator).
   284→                def _tc_to_dict(tc):
   285→                    if isinstance(tc, dict):
   286→                        return {
   287→                            "id": tc.get("id", f"call_{uuid.uuid4().hex[:8]}"),
   288→                            "type": "function",
   289→                            "function": {
   290→                                "name": tc.get("function", {}).get("name", tc.get("name", "")),
   291→                                "arguments": tc.get("function", {}).get("arguments", tc.get("arguments", "{}")),
   292→                            },
   293→                        }
   294→                    return {
   295→                        "id": tc.id,
   296→                        "type": "function",
   297→                        "function": {
   298→                            "name": tc.function.name,
   299→                            "arguments": tc.function.arguments,
   300→                        },
   301→                    }
   302→
   303→                # Build the assistant message dict for conversation history
   304→                msg_dict: Dict[str, Any] = {
   305→                    "role": "assistant",
   306→                    "content": assistant_msg.content or "",
   307→                    "tool_calls": [_tc_to_dict(tc) for tc in assistant_msg.tool_calls],
   308→                }
   309→
   310→                # Preserve reasoning_content for multi-turn chat template handling
   311→                # (e.g., Kimi-K2's template renders <think> blocks differently
   312→                # for history vs. the latest turn based on this field)
   313→                if reasoning:
   314→                    msg_dict["reasoning_content"] = reasoning
   315→
   316→                messages.append(msg_dict)
   317→
   318→                # Execute each tool call via hermes-agent's dispatch
   319→                for tc in assistant_msg.tool_calls:
   320→                    # Handle both object (OpenAI) and dict (vLLM) formats
   321→                    if isinstance(tc, dict):
   322→                        tool_name = tc.get("function", {}).get("name", tc.get("name", ""))
   323→                        tool_args_raw = tc.get("function", {}).get("arguments", tc.get("arguments", "{}"))
   324→                    else:
   325→                        tool_name = tc.function.name
   326→                        tool_args_raw = tc.function.arguments
   327→
   328→                    # Validate tool name
   329→                    if tool_name not in self.valid_tool_names:
   330→                        tool_result = json.dumps(
   331→                            {
   332→                                "error": f"Unknown tool '{tool_name}'. "
   333→                                f"Available tools: {sorted(self.valid_tool_names)}"
   334→                            }
   335→                        )
   336→                        tool_errors.append(ToolError(
   337→                            turn=turn + 1, tool_name=tool_name,
   338→                            arguments=tool_args_raw[:200],
   339→                            error=f"Unknown tool '{tool_name}'",
   340→                            tool_result=tool_result,
   341→                        ))
   342→                        logger.warning(
   343→                            "Model called unknown tool '%s' on turn %d",
   344→                            tool_name, turn + 1,
   345→                        )
   346→                    else:
   347→                        # Parse arguments and dispatch
   348→                        try:
   349→                            args = json.loads(tool_args_raw)
   350→                        except json.JSONDecodeError:
   351→                            args = {}
   352→                            logger.warning(
   353→                                "Invalid JSON in tool call arguments for '%s': %s",
   354→                                tool_name, tool_args_raw[:200],
   355→                            )
   356→
   357→                        try:
   358→                            if tool_name == "terminal":
   359→                                backend = os.getenv("TERMINAL_ENV", "local")
   360→                                cmd_preview = args.get("command", "")[:80]
   361→                                logger.info(
   362→                                    "[%s] $ %s", self.task_id[:8], cmd_preview,
   363→                                )
   364→
   365→                            tool_submit_time = _time.monotonic()
   366→
   367→                            # Todo tool -- handle locally (needs per-loop TodoStore)
   368→                            if tool_name == "todo":
   369→                                tool_result = _todo_tool(
   370→                                    todos=args.get("todos"),
   371→                                    merge=args.get("merge", False),
   372→                                    store=_todo_store,
   373→                                )
   374→                                tool_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - tool_submit_time
   375→                            elif tool_name == "memory":
   376→                                tool_result = json.dumps({"error": "Memory is not available in RL environments."})
   377→                                tool_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - tool_submit_time
   378→                            elif tool_name == "session_search":
   379→                                tool_result = json.dumps({"error": "Session search is not available in RL environments."})
   380→                                tool_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - tool_submit_time
   381→                            else:
   382→                                # Run tool calls in a thread pool so backends that
   383→                                # use asyncio.run() internally (modal, docker, daytona) get
   384→                                # a clean event loop instead of deadlocking.
   385→                                loop = asyncio.get_event_loop()
   386→                                # Capture current tool_name/args for the lambda
   387→                                _tn, _ta, _tid = tool_name, args, self.task_id
   388→                                tool_result = await loop.run_in_executor(
   389→                                    _tool_executor,
   390→                                    lambda: handle_function_call(
   391→                                        _tn, _ta, task_id=_tid,
   392→                                        user_task=_user_task,
   393→                                    ),
   394→                                )
   395→                                tool_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - tool_submit_time
   396→
   397→                            # Log slow tools and thread pool stats for debugging
   398→                            pool_active = _tool_executor._work_queue.qsize()
   399→                            if tool_elapsed > 30:
   400→                                logger.warning(
   401→                                    "[%s] turn %d: %s took %.1fs (pool queue=%d)",
   402→                                    self.task_id[:8], turn + 1, tool_name,
   403→                                    tool_elapsed, pool_active,
   404→                                )
   405→                        except Exception as e:
   406→                            tool_result = json.dumps(
   407→                                {"error": f"Tool execution failed: {type(e).__name__}: {str(e)}"}
   408→                            )
   409→                            tool_errors.append(ToolError(
   410→                                turn=turn + 1, tool_name=tool_name,
   411→                                arguments=tool_args_raw[:200],
   412→                                error=f"{type(e).__name__}: {str(e)}",
   413→                                tool_result=tool_result,
   414→                            ))
   415→                            logger.error(
   416→                                "Tool '%s' execution failed on turn %d: %s",
   417→                                tool_name, turn + 1, e,
   418→                            )
   419→
   420→                        # Also check if the tool returned an error in its JSON result
   421→                        try:
   422→                            result_data = json.loads(tool_result)
   423→                            if isinstance(result_data, dict):
   424→                                err = result_data.get("error")
   425→                                exit_code = result_data.get("exit_code")
   426→                                if err and exit_code and exit_code < 0:
   427→                                    tool_errors.append(ToolError(
   428→                                        turn=turn + 1, tool_name=tool_name,
   429→                                        arguments=tool_args_raw[:200],
   430→                                        error=str(err),
   431→                                        tool_result=tool_result[:500],
   432→                                    ))
   433→                        except (json.JSONDecodeError, TypeError):
   434→                            pass
   435→
   436→                    # Add tool response to conversation
   437→                    tc_id = tc.get("id", "") if isinstance(tc, dict) else tc.id
   438→                    messages.append(
   439→                        {
   440→                            "role": "tool",
   441→                            "tool_call_id": tc_id,
   442→                            "content": tool_result,
   443→                        }
   444→                    )
   445→
   446→                turn_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - turn_start
   447→                logger.info(
   448→                    "[%s] turn %d: api=%.1fs, %d tools, turn_total=%.1fs",
   449→                    self.task_id[:8], turn + 1, api_elapsed,
   450→                    len(assistant_msg.tool_calls), turn_elapsed,
   451→                )
   452→
   453→            else:
   454→                # No tool calls -- model is done
   455→                msg_dict = {
   456→                    "role": "assistant",
   457→                    "content": assistant_msg.content or "",
   458→                }
   459→                if reasoning:
   460→                    msg_dict["reasoning_content"] = reasoning
   461→                messages.append(msg_dict)
   462→
   463→                turn_elapsed = _time.monotonic() - turn_start
   464→                logger.info(
   465→                    "[%s] turn %d: api=%.1fs, no tools (finished), turn_total=%.1fs",
   466→                    self.task_id[:8], turn + 1, api_elapsed, turn_elapsed,
   467→                )
   468→
   469→                return AgentResult(
   470→                    messages=messages,
   471→                    managed_state=self._get_managed_state(),
   472→                    turns_used=turn + 1,
   473→                    finished_naturally=True,
   474→                    reasoning_per_turn=reasoning_per_turn,
   475→                    tool_errors=tool_errors,
   476→                )
   477→
   478→        # Hit max turns without the model stopping
   479→        logger.info("Agent hit max_turns (%d) without finishing", self.max_turns)
   480→        return AgentResult(
   481→            messages=messages,
   482→            managed_state=self._get_managed_state(),
   483→            turns_used=self.max_turns,
   484→            finished_naturally=False,
   485→            reasoning_per_turn=reasoning_per_turn,
   486→            tool_errors=tool_errors,
   487→        )
   488→
   489→    def _get_managed_state(self) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   490→        """
   491→        Get ManagedServer state if the server supports it.
   492→
   493→        Returns state dict with SequenceNodes containing tokens/logprobs/masks,
   494→        or None if the server doesn't support get_state() (e.g., regular OpenAI server).
   495→        """
   496→        if hasattr(self.server, "get_state"):
   497→            return self.server.get_state()
   498→        return None
   499→

   108→def model_ids() -> list[str]:

   112→
   113→def menu_labels() -> list[str]:
   114→    """Return display labels like 'anthropic/claude-opus-4.6 (recommended)'."""
   115→    labels = []
   116→    for mid, desc in OPENROUTER_MODELS:

   118→    return labels

   121→# All provider IDs and aliases that are valid for the provider:model syntax.
   122→_KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES: set[str] = (
   123→    set(_PROVIDER_LABELS.keys())
   124→    | set(_PROVIDER_ALIASES.keys())
   125→    | {"openrouter", "custom"}
   126→)

   129→def list_available_providers() -> list[dict[str, str]]:
   130→    """Return info about all providers the user could use with ``provider:model``.
   131→
   132→    Each dict has ``id``, ``label``, and ``aliases``.
   133→    Checks which providers have valid credentials configured.
   134→    """
   135→    # Canonical providers in display order
   136→    _PROVIDER_ORDER = [
   137→        "openrouter", "nous", "openai-codex",
   138→        "zai", "kimi-coding", "minimax", "minimax-cn", "anthropic",
   139→    ]
   140→    # Build reverse alias map
   141→    aliases_for: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
   142→    for alias, canonical in _PROVIDER_ALIASES.items():
   143→        aliases_for.setdefault(canonical, []).append(alias)
   144→
   145→    result = []
   146→    for pid in _PROVIDER_ORDER:
   147→        label = _PROVIDER_LABELS.get(pid, pid)
   148→        alias_list = aliases_for.get(pid, [])
   149→        # Check if this provider has credentials available
   150→        has_creds = False
   151→        try:
   152→            from hermes_cli.runtime_provider import resolve_runtime_provider
   153→            runtime = resolve_runtime_provider(requested=pid)
   154→            has_creds = bool(runtime.get("api_key"))
   155→        except Exception:
   156→            pass
   157→        result.append({
   158→            "id": pid,
   159→            "label": label,
   160→            "aliases": alias_list,
   161→            "authenticated": has_creds,
   162→        })
   163→    return result
   164→
   165→
   166→def parse_model_input(raw: str, current_provider: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
   167→    """Parse ``/model`` input into ``(provider, model)``.
   168→
   169→    Supports ``provider:model`` syntax to switch providers at runtime::
   170→
   171→        openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5  →  ("openrouter", "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5")
   172→        nous:hermes-3                           →  ("nous", "hermes-3")
   173→        anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5             →  (current_provider, "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5")
   174→        gpt-5.4                                 →  (current_provider, "gpt-5.4")
   175→
   176→    The colon is only treated as a provider delimiter if the left side is a
   177→    recognized provider name or alias.  This avoids misinterpreting model names
   178→    that happen to contain colons (e.g. ``anthropic/claude-3.5-sonnet:beta``).
   179→
   180→    Returns ``(provider, model)`` where *provider* is either the explicit
   181→    provider from the input or *current_provider* if none was specified.
   182→    """
   183→    stripped = raw.strip()
   184→    colon = stripped.find(":")
   185→    if colon > 0:
   186→        provider_part = stripped[:colon].strip().lower()
   187→        model_part = stripped[colon + 1:].strip()
   188→        if provider_part and model_part and provider_part in _KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES:
   189→            return (normalize_provider(provider_part), model_part)
   190→    return (current_provider, stripped)
   191→
   192→
   193→def curated_models_for_provider(provider: Optional[str]) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
   194→    """Return ``(model_id, description)`` tuples for a provider's model list.
   195→
   196→    Tries to fetch the live model list from the provider's API first,
   197→    falling back to the static ``_PROVIDER_MODELS`` catalog if the API
   198→    is unreachable.
   199→    """
   200→    normalized = normalize_provider(provider)
   201→    if normalized == "openrouter":
   202→        return list(OPENROUTER_MODELS)
   203→
   204→    # Try live API first (Codex, Nous, etc. all support /models)
   205→    live = provider_model_ids(normalized)
   206→    if live:
   207→        return [(m, "") for m in live]
   208→
   209→    # Fallback to static catalog
   210→    models = _PROVIDER_MODELS.get(normalized, [])
   211→    return [(m, "") for m in models]
   212→
   213→
   214→def normalize_provider(provider: Optional[str]) -> str:
   215→    """Normalize provider aliases to Hermes' canonical provider ids.
   216→
   217→    Note: ``"auto"`` passes through unchanged — use
   218→    ``hermes_cli.auth.resolve_provider()`` to resolve it to a concrete
   219→    provider based on credentials and environment.
   220→    """
   221→    normalized = (provider or "openrouter").strip().lower()
   222→    return _PROVIDER_ALIASES.get(normalized, normalized)
   223→
   224→
   225→def provider_model_ids(provider: Optional[str]) -> list[str]:
   226→    """Return the best known model catalog for a provider.
   227→
   228→    Tries live API endpoints for providers that support them (Codex, Nous),
   229→    falling back to static lists.
   230→    """
   231→    normalized = normalize_provider(provider)
   232→    if normalized == "openrouter":
   233→        return model_ids()
   234→    if normalized == "openai-codex":
   235→        from hermes_cli.codex_models import get_codex_model_ids
   236→
   237→        return get_codex_model_ids()
   238→    if normalized == "nous":
   239→        # Try live Nous Portal /models endpoint
   240→        try:
   241→            from hermes_cli.auth import fetch_nous_models, resolve_nous_runtime_credentials
   242→            creds = resolve_nous_runtime_credentials()
   243→            if creds:
   244→                live = fetch_nous_models(creds.get("api_key", ""), creds.get("base_url", ""))
   245→                if live:
   246→                    return live
   247→        except Exception:
   248→            pass
   249→    if normalized == "anthropic":
   250→        live = _fetch_anthropic_models()
   251→        if live:
   252→            return live
   253→    return list(_PROVIDER_MODELS.get(normalized, []))
   254→
   255→
   256→def _fetch_anthropic_models(timeout: float = 5.0) -> Optional[list[str]]:
   257→    """Fetch available models from the Anthropic /v1/models endpoint.
   258→
   259→    Uses resolve_anthropic_token() to find credentials (env vars or
   260→    Claude Code auto-discovery).  Returns sorted model IDs or None.
   261→    """
   262→    try:
   263→        from agent.anthropic_adapter import resolve_anthropic_token, _is_oauth_token
   264→    except ImportError:
   265→        return None
   266→
   267→    [REDACTED]()
   268→    if not token:
   269→        return None
   270→
   271→    headers: dict[str, str] = {"anthropic-version": "2023-06-01"}
   272→    if _is_oauth_token(token):
   273→        headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {token}"
   274→        from agent.anthropic_adapter import _COMMON_BETAS, _OAUTH_ONLY_BETAS
   275→        headers["anthropic-beta"] = ",".join(_COMMON_BETAS + _OAUTH_ONLY_BETAS)
   276→    else:
   277→        headers["x-api-key"] = token
   278→
   279→    req = urllib.request.Request(
   280→        "https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models",
   281→        headers=headers,
   282→    )
   283→    try:
   284→        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as resp:
   285→            data = json.loads(resp.read().decode())
   286→            models = [m["id"] for m in data.get("data", []) if m.get("id")]
   287→            # Sort: latest/largest first (opus > sonnet > haiku, higher version first)
   288→            return sorted(models, key=lambda m: (
   289→                "opus" not in m,      # opus first
   290→                "sonnet" not in m,    # then sonnet
   291→                "haiku" not in m,     # then haiku
   292→                m,                    # alphabetical within tier
   293→            ))
   294→    except Exception as e:
   295→        import logging
   296→        logging.getLogger(__name__).debug("Failed to fetch Anthropic models: %s", e)
   297→        return None
   298→
   299→
   300→def fetch_api_models(
   301→    api_key: Optional[str],
   302→    base_url: Optional[str],
   303→    timeout: float = 5.0,
   304→) -> Optional[list[str]]:
   305→    """Fetch the list of available model IDs from the provider's ``/models`` endpoint.
   306→
   307→    Returns a list of model ID strings, or ``None`` if the endpoint could not
   308→    be reached (network error, timeout, auth failure, etc.).
   309→    """
   310→    if not base_url:
   311→        return None
   312→
   313→    url = base_url.rstrip("/") + "/models"
   314→    headers: dict[str, str] = {}
   315→    if api_key:
   316→        headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {api_key}"
   317→
   318→    req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers=headers)
   319→    try:
   320→        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as resp:
   321→            data = json.loads(resp.read().decode())
   322→            # Standard OpenAI format: {"data": [{"id": "model-name", ...}, ...]}
   323→            return [m.get("id", "") for m in data.get("data", [])]
   324→    except Exception:
   325→        return None
   326→
   327→
   328→def validate_requested_model(
   329→    model_name: str,
   330→    provider: Optional[str],
   331→    *,
   332→    api_key: Optional[str] = None,
   333→    base_url: Optional[str] = None,
   334→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   335→    """
   336→    Validate a ``/model`` value for the active provider.
   337→
   338→    Performs format checks first, then probes the live API to confirm
   339→    the model actually exists.
   340→
   341→    Returns a dict with:
   342→      - accepted: whether the CLI should switch to the requested model now
   343→      - persist: whether it is safe to save to config
   344→      - recognized: whether it matched a known provider catalog
   345→      - message: optional warning / guidance for the user
   346→    """
   347→    requested = (model_name or "").strip()
   348→    normalized = normalize_provider(provider)
   349→    if normalized == "openrouter" and base_url and "openrouter.ai" not in base_url:
   350→        normalized = "custom"
   351→
   352→    if not requested:
   353→        return {
   354→            "accepted": False,
   355→            "persist": False,
   356→            "recognized": False,
   357→            "message": "Model name cannot be empty.",
   358→        }
   359→
   360→    if any(ch.isspace() for ch in requested):
   361→        return {
   362→            "accepted": False,
   363→            "persist": False,
   364→            "recognized": False,
   365→            "message": "Model names cannot contain spaces.",
   366→        }
   367→
   368→    # Custom endpoints can serve any model — skip validation
   369→    if normalized == "custom":
   370→        return {
   371→            "accepted": True,
   372→            "persist": True,
   373→            "recognized": False,
   374→            "message": None,
   375→        }
   376→
   377→    # Probe the live API to check if the model actually exists
   378→    api_models = fetch_api_models(api_key, base_url)
   379→
   380→    if api_models is not None:
   381→        if requested in set(api_models):
   382→            # API confirmed the model exists
   383→            return {
   384→                "accepted": True,
   385→                "persist": True,
   386→                "recognized": True,
   387→                "message": None,
   388→            }
   389→        else:
   390→            # API responded but model is not listed.  Accept anyway —
   391→            # the user may have access to models not shown in the public
   392→            # listing (e.g. Z.AI Pro/Max plans can use glm-5 on coding
   393→            # endpoints even though it's not in /models).  Warn but allow.
   394→            suggestions = get_close_matches(requested, api_models, n=3, cutoff=0.5)
   395→            suggestion_text = ""
   396→            if suggestions:
   397→                suggestion_text = "\n  Similar models: " + ", ".join(f"`{s}`" for s in suggestions)
   398→
   399→            return {
   400→                "accepted": True,
   401→                "persist": True,
   402→                "recognized": False,
   403→                "message": (
   404→                    f"Note: `{requested}` was not found in this provider's model listing. "
   405→                    f"It may still work if your plan supports it."
   406→                    f"{suggestion_text}"
   407→                ),
   408→            }
   409→
   410→    # api_models is None — couldn't reach API.  Accept and persist,
   411→    # but warn so typos don't silently break things.
   412→    provider_label = _PROVIDER_LABELS.get(normalized, normalized)
   413→    return {
   414→        "accepted": True,
   415→        "persist": True,
   416→        "recognized": False,
   417→        "message": (
   418→            f"Could not reach the {provider_label} API to validate `{requested}`. "
   419→            f"If the service isn't down, this model may not be valid."
   420→        ),
   421→    }
   422→

1→"""Model metadata, context lengths, and token estimation utilities.
     2→
     3→Pure utility functions with no AIAgent dependency. Used by ContextCompressor
     4→and run_agent.py for pre-flight context checks.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→import logging

    11→from pathlib import Path
    12→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional
    13→

    15→import yaml
    16→
    17→from hermes_constants import OPENROUTER_MODELS_URL
    18→
    19→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    20→
    21→_model_metadata_cache: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]] = {}
    22→_model_metadata_cache_time: float = 0
    23→_MODEL_CACHE_TTL = 3600
    24→
    25→# Descending tiers for context length probing when the model is unknown.
    26→# We start high and step down on context-length errors until one works.
    27→CONTEXT_PROBE_TIERS = [
    28→    2_000_000,
    29→    1_000_000,
    30→    512_000,
    31→    200_000,
    32→    128_000,
    33→    64_000,
    34→    32_000,
    35→]
    36→
    37→DEFAULT_CONTEXT_LENGTHS = {
    38→    "anthropic/claude-opus-4": 200000,
    39→    "anthropic/claude-opus-4.5": 200000,
    40→    "anthropic/claude-opus-4.6": 200000,

    42→    "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4-20250514": 200000,
    43→    "anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5": 200000,

    56→    "google/gemini-2.0-flash": 1048576,
    57→    "google/gemini-2.5-pro": 1048576,
    58→    "meta-llama/llama-3.3-70b-instruct": 131072,
    59→    "deepseek/deepseek-chat-v3": 65536,
    60→    "qwen/qwen-2.5-72b-instruct": 32768,
    61→    "glm-4.7": 202752,
    62→    "glm-5": 202752,
    63→    "glm-4.5": 131072,
    64→    "glm-4.5-flash": 131072,

    66→    "kimi-k2.5": 262144,
    67→    "kimi-k2-thinking": 262144,
    68→    "kimi-k2-thinking-turbo": 262144,

    73→    "MiniMax-M2.1": 204800,

    76→
    77→def fetch_model_metadata(force_refresh: bool = False) -> Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]]:
    78→    """Fetch model metadata from OpenRouter (cached for 1 hour)."""
    79→    global _model_metadata_cache, _model_metadata_cache_time
    80→
    81→    if not force_refresh and _model_metadata_cache and (time.time() - _model_metadata_cache_time) < _MODEL_CACHE_TTL:
    82→        return _model_metadata_cache
    83→
    84→    try:
    85→        response = requests.get(OPENROUTER_MODELS_URL, timeout=10)
    86→        response.raise_for_status()
    87→        data = response.json()
    88→
    89→        cache = {}
    90→        for model in data.get("data", []):
    91→            model_id = model.get("id", "")
    92→            cache[model_id] = {
    93→                "context_length": model.get("context_length", 128000),
    94→                "max_completion_tokens": model.get("top_provider", {}).get("max_completion_tokens", 4096),
    95→                "name": model.get("name", model_id),
    96→                "pricing": model.get("pricing", {}),
    97→            }
    98→            canonical = model.get("canonical_slug", "")
    99→            if canonical and canonical != model_id:
   100→                cache[canonical] = cache[model_id]
   101→
   102→        _model_metadata_cache = cache
   103→        _model_metadata_cache_time = time.time()
   104→        logger.debug("Fetched metadata for %s models from OpenRouter", len(cache))
   105→        return cache
   106→
   107→    except Exception as e:
   108→        logging.warning(f"Failed to fetch model metadata from OpenRouter: {e}")
   109→        return _model_metadata_cache or {}
   110→
   111→
   112→def _get_context_cache_path() -> Path:
   113→    """Return path to the persistent context length cache file."""
   114→    hermes_home = Path(os.environ.get("HERMES_HOME", Path.home() / ".hermes"))
   115→    return hermes_home / "context_length_cache.yaml"
   116→
   117→
   118→def _load_context_cache() -> Dict[str, int]:
   119→    """Load the model+provider → context_length cache from disk."""
   120→    path = _get_context_cache_path()
   121→    if not path.exists():
   122→        return {}
   123→    try:
   124→        with open(path) as f:
   125→            data = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
   126→        return data.get("context_lengths", {})
   127→    except Exception as e:
   128→        logger.debug("Failed to load context length cache: %s", e)
   129→        return {}
   130→
   131→
   132→def save_context_length(model: str, base_url: str, length: int) -> None:
   133→    """Persist a discovered context length for a model+provider combo.
   134→
   135→    Cache key is ``model@base_url`` so the same model name served from
   136→    different providers can have different limits.
   137→    """
   138→    key = f"{model}@{base_url}"
   139→    cache = _load_context_cache()
   140→    if cache.get(key) == length:
   141→        return  # already stored
   142→    cache[key] = length
   143→    path = _get_context_cache_path()
   144→    try:
   145→        path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   146→        with open(path, "w") as f:
   147→            yaml.dump({"context_lengths": cache}, f, default_flow_style=False)
   148→        logger.info("Cached context length %s → %s tokens", key, f"{length:,}")
   149→    except Exception as e:
   150→        logger.debug("Failed to save context length cache: %s", e)
   151→
   152→
   153→def get_cached_context_length(model: str, base_url: str) -> Optional[int]:
   154→    """Look up a previously discovered context length for model+provider."""
   155→    key = f"{model}@{base_url}"
   156→    cache = _load_context_cache()
   157→    return cache.get(key)
   158→
   159→
   160→def get_next_probe_tier(current_length: int) -> Optional[int]:
   161→    """Return the next lower probe tier, or None if already at minimum."""
   162→    for tier in CONTEXT_PROBE_TIERS:
   163→        if tier < current_length:
   164→            return tier
   165→    return None
   166→
   167→
   168→def parse_context_limit_from_error(error_msg: str) -> Optional[int]:
   169→    """Try to extract the actual context limit from an API error message.
   170→
   171→    Many providers include the limit in their error text, e.g.:
   172→      - "maximum context length is 32768 tokens"
   173→      - "context_length_exceeded: 131072"
   174→      - "Maximum context size 32768 exceeded"
   175→      - "model's max context length is 65536"
   176→    """
   177→    error_lower = error_msg.lower()
   178→    # Pattern: look for numbers near context-related keywords
   179→    patterns = [
   180→        r'(?:max(?:imum)?|limit)\s*(?:context\s*)?(?:length|size|window)?\s*(?:is|of|:)?\s*(\d{4,})',
   181→        r'context\s*(?:length|size|window)\s*(?:is|of|:)?\s*(\d{4,})',
   182→        r'(\d{4,})\s*(?:token)?\s*(?:context|limit)',
   183→        r'>\s*(\d{4,})\s*(?:max|limit|token)',  # "250000 tokens > 200000 maximum"
   184→        r'(\d{4,})\s*(?:max(?:imum)?)\b',  # "200000 maximum"
   185→    ]
   186→    for pattern in patterns:
   187→        match = re.search(pattern, error_lower)
   188→        if match:
   189→            limit = int(match.group(1))
   190→            # Sanity check: must be a reasonable context length
   191→            if 1024 <= limit <= 10_000_000:
   192→                return limit
   193→    return None
   194→
   195→
   196→def get_model_context_length(model: str, base_url: str = "") -> int:
   197→    """Get the context length for a model.
   198→
   199→    Resolution order:
   200→    1. Persistent cache (previously discovered via probing)
   201→    2. OpenRouter API metadata
   202→    3. Hardcoded DEFAULT_CONTEXT_LENGTHS (fuzzy match)
   203→    4. First probe tier (2M) — will be narrowed on first context error
   204→    """
   205→    # 1. Check persistent cache (model+provider)
   206→    if base_url:
   207→        cached = get_cached_context_length(model, base_url)
   208→        if cached is not None:
   209→            return cached
   210→
   211→    # 2. OpenRouter API metadata
   212→    metadata = fetch_model_metadata()
   213→    if model in metadata:
   214→        return metadata[model].get("context_length", 128000)
   215→
   216→    # 3. Hardcoded defaults (fuzzy match)
   217→    for default_model, length in DEFAULT_CONTEXT_LENGTHS.items():
   218→        if default_model in model or model in default_model:
   219→            return length
   220→
   221→    # 4. Unknown model — start at highest probe tier
   222→    return CONTEXT_PROBE_TIERS[0]
   223→
   224→
   225→def estimate_tokens_rough(text: str) -> int:
   226→    """Rough token estimate (~4 chars/token) for pre-flight checks."""
   227→    if not text:
   228→        return 0
   229→    return len(text) // 4
   230→
   231→
   232→def estimate_messages_tokens_rough(messages: List[Dict[str, Any]]) -> int:
   233→    """Rough token estimate for a message list (pre-flight only)."""
   234→    total_chars = sum(len(str(msg)) for msg in messages)
   235→    return total_chars // 4
   236→

   112→class _SafeWriter:
   113→    """Transparent stdout wrapper that catches OSError from broken pipes.
   114→
   115→    When hermes-agent runs as a systemd service, Docker container, or headless
   116→    daemon, the stdout pipe can become unavailable (idle timeout, buffer
   117→    exhaustion, socket reset). Any print() call then raises
   118→    ``OSError: [Errno 5] Input/output error``, which can crash
   119→    run_conversation() — especially via double-fault when the except handler
   120→    also tries to print.
   121→
   122→    This wrapper delegates all writes to the underlying stream and silently
   123→    catches OSError.  It is installed once at the start of run_conversation()
   124→    and is transparent when stdout is healthy (zero overhead on the happy path).
   125→    """
   126→
   127→    __slots__ = ("_inner",)
   128→
   129→    def __init__(self, inner):
   130→        object.__setattr__(self, "_inner", inner)
   131→
   132→    def write(self, data):
   133→        try:
   134→            return self._inner.write(data)
   135→        except OSError:
   136→            return len(data) if isinstance(data, str) else 0
   137→
   138→    def flush(self):
   139→        try:
   140→            self._inner.flush()
   141→        except OSError:
   142→            pass
   143→
   144→    def fileno(self):
   145→        return self._inner.fileno()
   146→
   147→    def isatty(self):
   148→        try:
   149→            return self._inner.isatty()
   150→        except OSError:

1→"""Shared runtime provider resolution for CLI, gateway, cron, and helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→from typing import Any, Dict, Optional
     7→
     8→from hermes_cli.auth import (
     9→    AuthError,
    10→    PROVIDER_REGISTRY,
    11→    format_auth_error,
    12→    resolve_provider,
    13→    resolve_nous_runtime_credentials,
    14→    resolve_codex_runtime_credentials,
    15→    resolve_api_key_provider_credentials,
    16→)
    17→from hermes_cli.config import load_config
    18→from hermes_constants import OPENROUTER_BASE_URL
    19→
    20→
    21→def _get_model_config() -> Dict[str, Any]:
    22→    config = load_config()
    23→    model_cfg = config.get("model")
    24→    if isinstance(model_cfg, dict):
    25→        return dict(model_cfg)
    26→    if isinstance(model_cfg, str) and model_cfg.strip():
    27→        return {"default": model_cfg.strip()}
    28→    return {}
    29→
    30→
    31→def resolve_requested_provider(requested: Optional[str] = None) -> str:
    32→    """Resolve provider request from explicit arg, env, then config."""
    33→    if requested and requested.strip():
    34→        return requested.strip().lower()
    35→
    36→    env_provider = os.getenv("HERMES_INFERENCE_PROVIDER", "").strip().lower()
    37→    if env_provider:
    38→        return env_provider
    39→
    40→    model_cfg = _get_model_config()
    41→    cfg_provider = model_cfg.get("provider")
    42→    if isinstance(cfg_provider, str) and cfg_provider.strip():
    43→        return cfg_provider.strip().lower()
    44→
    45→    return "auto"
    46→
    47→
    48→def _resolve_openrouter_runtime(
    49→    *,
    50→    requested_provider: str,
    51→    explicit_api_key: Optional[str] = None,
    52→    explicit_base_url: Optional[str] = None,
    53→) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    54→    model_cfg = _get_model_config()
    55→    cfg_base_url = model_cfg.get("base_url") if isinstance(model_cfg.get("base_url"), str) else ""
    56→    cfg_provider = model_cfg.get("provider") if isinstance(model_cfg.get("provider"), str) else ""
    57→    requested_norm = (requested_provider or "").strip().lower()
    58→    cfg_provider = cfg_provider.strip().lower()
    59→
    60→    env_openai_base_url = os.getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL", "").strip()
    61→    env_openrouter_base_url = os.getenv("OPENROUTER_BASE_URL", "").strip()
    62→
    63→    use_config_base_url = False
    64→    if requested_norm == "auto":
    65→        if cfg_base_url.strip() and not explicit_base_url and not env_openai_base_url:
    66→            if not cfg_provider or cfg_provider == "auto":
    67→                use_config_base_url = True
    68→
    69→    # When the user explicitly requested the openrouter provider, skip
    70→    # OPENAI_BASE_URL — it typically points to a custom / non-OpenRouter
    71→    # endpoint and would prevent switching back to OpenRouter (#874).
    72→    skip_openai_base = requested_norm == "openrouter"
    73→
    74→    base_url = (
    75→        (explicit_base_url or "").strip()
    76→        or ("" if skip_openai_base else env_openai_base_url)
    77→        or (cfg_base_url.strip() if use_config_base_url else "")
    78→        or env_openrouter_base_url
    79→        or OPENROUTER_BASE_URL
    80→    ).rstrip("/")
    81→
    82→    # Choose API key based on whether the resolved base_url targets OpenRouter.
    83→    # When hitting OpenRouter, prefer OPENROUTER_API_KEY (issue #289).
    84→    # When hitting a custom endpoint (e.g. Z.ai, local LLM), prefer
    85→    # OPENAI_API_KEY so the OpenRouter key doesn't leak to an unrelated
    86→    # provider (issues #420, #560).
    87→    _is_openrouter_url = "openrouter.ai" in base_url
    88→    if _is_openrouter_url:
    89→        api_key = (
    90→            explicit_api_key
    91→            or os.getenv("OPENROUTER_API_KEY")
    92→            or os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY")
    93→            or ""
    94→        )
    95→    else:
    96→        api_key = (
    97→            explicit_api_key
    98→            or os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY")
    99→            or os.getenv("OPENROUTER_API_KEY")
   100→            or ""
   101→        )
   102→
   103→    source = "explicit" if (explicit_api_key or explicit_base_url) else "env/config"
   104→
   105→    return {
   106→        "provider": "openrouter",
   107→        "api_mode": "chat_completions",
   108→        "base_url": base_url,
   109→        "api_key": api_key,
   110→        "source": source,
   111→    }
   112→
   113→
   114→def resolve_runtime_provider(
   115→    *,
   116→    requested: Optional[str] = None,
   117→    explicit_api_key: Optional[str] = None,
   118→    explicit_base_url: Optional[str] = None,
   119→) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   120→    """Resolve runtime provider credentials for agent execution."""
   121→    requested_provider = resolve_requested_provider(requested)
   122→
   123→    provider = resolve_provider(
   124→        requested_provider,
   125→        [REDACTED],
   126→        explicit_base_url=explicit_base_url,
   127→    )
   128→
   129→    if provider == "nous":
   130→        creds = resolve_nous_runtime_credentials(
   131→            min_key_ttl_seconds=max(60, int(os.getenv("HERMES_NOUS_MIN_KEY_TTL_SECONDS", "1800"))),
   132→            timeout_seconds=float(os.getenv("HERMES_NOUS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS", "15")),
   133→        )
   134→        return {
   135→            "provider": "nous",
   136→            "api_mode": "chat_completions",
   137→            "base_url": creds.get("base_url", "").rstrip("/"),
   138→            "api_key": creds.get("api_key", ""),
   139→            "source": creds.get("source", "portal"),
   140→            "expires_at": creds.get("expires_at"),
   141→            "requested_provider": requested_provider,
   142→        }
   143→
   144→    if provider == "openai-codex":
   145→        creds = resolve_codex_runtime_credentials()
   146→        return {
   147→            "provider": "openai-codex",
   148→            "api_mode": "codex_responses",
   149→            "base_url": creds.get("base_url", "").rstrip("/"),
   150→            "api_key": creds.get("api_key", ""),
   151→            "source": creds.get("source", "hermes-auth-store"),
   152→            "last_refresh": creds.get("last_refresh"),
   153→            "requested_provider": requested_provider,
   154→        }
   155→
   156→    # Anthropic (native Messages API)
   157→    if provider == "anthropic":
   158→        from agent.anthropic_adapter import resolve_anthropic_token
   159→        [REDACTED]()
   160→        if not token:
   161→            raise AuthError(
   162→                "No Anthropic credentials found. Set ANTHROPIC_TOKEN or ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, "
   163→                "run 'claude setup-token', or authenticate with 'claude /login'."
   164→            )
   165→        return {
   166→            "provider": "anthropic",
   167→            "api_mode": "anthropic_messages",
   168→            "base_url": "https://api.anthropic.com",
   169→            "api_key": token,
   170→            "source": "env",
   171→            "requested_provider": requested_provider,
   172→        }
   173→
   174→    # API-key providers (z.ai/GLM, Kimi, MiniMax, MiniMax-CN)
   175→    pconfig = PROVIDER_REGISTRY.get(provider)
   176→    if pconfig and pconfig.auth_type == "api_key":
   177→        creds = resolve_api_key_provider_credentials(provider)
   178→        return {
   179→            "provider": provider,
   180→            "api_mode": "chat_completions",
   181→            "base_url": creds.get("base_url", "").rstrip("/"),
   182→            "api_key": creds.get("api_key", ""),
   183→            "source": creds.get("source", "env"),
   184→            "requested_provider": requested_provider,
   185→        }
   186→
   187→    runtime = _resolve_openrouter_runtime(
   188→        requested_provider=requested_provider,
   189→        [REDACTED],
   190→        explicit_base_url=explicit_base_url,
   191→    )
   192→    runtime["requested_provider"] = requested_provider
   193→    return runtime
   194→
   195→
   196→def format_runtime_provider_error(error: Exception) -> str:
   197→    if isinstance(error, AuthError):
   198→        return format_auth_error(error)
   199→    return str(error)
   200→

1→#!/usr/bin/env python3
     2→"""
     3→Model Tools Module
     4→
     5→Thin orchestration layer over the tool registry. Each tool file in tools/
     6→self-registers its schema, handler, and metadata via tools.registry.register().
     7→This module triggers discovery (by importing all tool modules), then provides
     8→the public API that run_agent.py, cli.py, batch_runner.py, and the RL
     9→environments consume.
    10→
    11→Public API (signatures preserved from the original 2,400-line version):
    12→    get_tool_definitions(enabled_toolsets, disabled_toolsets, quiet_mode) -> list
    13→    handle_function_call(function_name, function_args, task_id, user_task) -> str
    14→    TOOL_TO_TOOLSET_MAP: dict          (for batch_runner.py)
    15→    TOOLSET_REQUIREMENTS: dict         (for cli.py, doctor.py)
    16→    get_all_tool_names() -> list
    17→    get_toolset_for_tool(name) -> str
    18→    get_available_toolsets() -> dict
    19→    check_toolset_requirements() -> dict
    20→    check_tool_availability(quiet) -> tuple
    21→"""
    22→
    23→import json
    24→import asyncio
    25→import os
    26→import logging

    28→

    32→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    33→
    34→
    35→# =============================================================================
    36→# Async Bridging  (single source of truth -- used by registry.dispatch too)
    37→# =============================================================================
    38→
    39→def _run_async(coro):
    40→    """Run an async coroutine from a sync context.
    41→
    42→    If the current thread already has a running event loop (e.g., inside
    43→    the gateway's async stack or Atropos's event loop), we spin up a
    44→    disposable thread so asyncio.run() can create its own loop without
    45→    conflicting.
    46→
    47→    This is the single source of truth for sync->async bridging in tool
    48→    handlers. The RL paths (agent_loop.py, tool_context.py) also provide
    49→    outer thread-pool wrapping as defense-in-depth, but each handler is
    50→    self-protecting via this function.
    51→    """
    52→    try:
    53→        loop = asyncio.get_running_loop()
    54→    except RuntimeError:
    55→        loop = None
    56→
    57→    if loop and loop.is_running():
    58→        import concurrent.futures
    59→        with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=1) as pool:
    60→            future = pool.submit(asyncio.run, coro)
    61→            return future.result(timeout=300)
    62→    return asyncio.run(coro)
    63→
    64→
    65→# =============================================================================
    66→# Tool Discovery  (importing each module triggers its registry.register calls)
    67→# =============================================================================
    68→
    69→def _discover_tools():
    70→    """Import all tool modules to trigger their registry.register() calls.
    71→
    72→    Wrapped in a function so import errors in optional tools (e.g., fal_client
    73→    not installed) don't prevent the rest from loading.
    74→    """
    75→    _modules = [
    76→        "tools.web_tools",
    77→        "tools.terminal_tool",
    78→        "tools.file_tools",
    79→        "tools.vision_tools",
    80→        "tools.mixture_of_agents_tool",
    81→        "tools.image_generation_tool",
    82→        "tools.skills_tool",
    83→        "tools.skill_manager_tool",
    84→        "tools.browser_tool",
    85→        "tools.cronjob_tools",
    86→        "tools.rl_training_tool",
    87→        "tools.tts_tool",
    88→        "tools.todo_tool",
    89→        "tools.memory_tool",
    90→        "tools.session_search_tool",
    91→        "tools.clarify_tool",
    92→        "tools.code_execution_tool",
    93→        "tools.delegate_tool",
    94→        "tools.process_registry",
    95→        "tools.send_message_tool",
    96→        "tools.honcho_tools",
    97→        "tools.homeassistant_tool",
    98→    ]
    99→    import importlib
   100→    for mod_name in _modules:
   101→        try:
   102→            importlib.import_module(mod_name)
   103→        except Exception as e:
   104→            logger.debug("Could not import %s: %s", mod_name, e)
   105→

   115→

   119→# =============================================================================
   120→
   121→TOOL_TO_TOOLSET_MAP: Dict[str, str] = registry.get_tool_to_toolset_map()
   122→
   123→TOOLSET_REQUIREMENTS: Dict[str, dict] = registry.get_toolset_requirements()
   124→
   125→# Resolved tool names from the last get_tool_definitions() call.
   126→# Used by code_execution_tool to know which tools are available in this session.
   127→_last_resolved_tool_names: List[str] = []
   128→
   129→
   130→# =============================================================================
   131→# Legacy toolset name mapping  (old _tools-suffixed names -> tool name lists)
   132→# =============================================================================
   133→
   134→_LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP = {
   135→    "web_tools": ["web_search", "web_extract"],
   136→    "terminal_tools": ["terminal"],
   137→    "vision_tools": ["vision_analyze"],
   138→    "moa_tools": ["mixture_of_agents"],
   139→    "image_tools": ["image_generate"],
   140→    "skills_tools": ["skills_list", "skill_view", "skill_manage"],
   141→    "browser_tools": [
   142→        "browser_navigate", "browser_snapshot", "browser_click",
   143→        "browser_type", "browser_scroll", "browser_back",
   144→        "browser_press", "browser_close", "browser_get_images",
   145→        "browser_vision"
   146→    ],
   147→    "cronjob_tools": ["schedule_cronjob", "list_cronjobs", "remove_cronjob"],
   148→    "rl_tools": [
   149→        "rl_list_environments", "rl_select_environment",
   150→        "rl_get_current_config", "rl_edit_config",
   151→        "rl_start_training", "rl_check_status",
   152→        "rl_stop_training", "rl_get_results",
   153→        "rl_list_runs", "rl_test_inference"
   154→    ],
   155→    "file_tools": ["read_file", "write_file", "patch", "search_files"],
   156→    "tts_tools": ["text_to_speech"],
   157→}
   158→
   159→
   160→# =============================================================================
   161→# get_tool_definitions  (the main schema provider)
   162→# =============================================================================
   163→
   164→def get_tool_definitions(
   165→    enabled_toolsets: List[str] = None,
   166→    disabled_toolsets: List[str] = None,
   167→    quiet_mode: bool = False,
   168→) -> List[Dict[str, Any]]:
   169→    """
   170→    Get tool definitions for model API calls with toolset-based filtering.
   171→
   172→    All tools must be part of a toolset to be accessible.
   173→
   174→    Args:
   175→        enabled_toolsets: Only include tools from these toolsets.
   176→        disabled_toolsets: Exclude tools from these toolsets (if enabled_toolsets is None).
   177→        quiet_mode: Suppress status prints.
   178→
   179→    Returns:
   180→        Filtered list of OpenAI-format tool definitions.
   181→    """
   182→    # Determine which tool names the caller wants
   183→    tools_to_include: set = set()
   184→
   185→    if enabled_toolsets:
   186→        for toolset_name in enabled_toolsets:
   187→            if validate_toolset(toolset_name):
   188→                resolved = resolve_toolset(toolset_name)
   189→                tools_to_include.update(resolved)
   190→                if not quiet_mode:
   191→                    print(f"✅ Enabled toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(resolved) if resolved else 'no tools'}")
   192→            elif toolset_name in _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP:
   193→                legacy_tools = _LEGACY_TOOLSET_MAP[toolset_name]
   194→                tools_to_include.update(legacy_tools)
   195→                if not quiet_mode:
   196→                    print(f"✅ Enabled legacy toolset '{toolset_name}': {', '.join(legacy_tools)}")
   197→            else:
   198→                if not quiet_mode:
   199→                    print(f"⚠️  Unknown toolset: {toolset_name}")
   200→

   224→
   225→    # Ask the registry for schemas (only returns tools whose check_fn passes)
   226→    filtered_tools = registry.get_definitions(tools_to_include, quiet=quiet_mode)
   227→
   228→    # Rebuild execute_code schema to only list sandbox tools that are actually
   229→    # enabled.  Without this, the model sees "web_search is available in
   230→    # execute_code" even when the user disabled the web toolset (#560-discord).
   231→    if "execute_code" in tools_to_include:
   232→        from tools.code_execution_tool import SANDBOX_ALLOWED_TOOLS, build_execute_code_schema
   233→        sandbox_enabled = SANDBOX_ALLOWED_TOOLS & tools_to_include
   234→        dynamic_schema = build_execute_code_schema(sandbox_enabled)
   235→        for i, td in enumerate(filtered_tools):
   236→            if td.get("function", {}).get("name") == "execute_code":
   237→                filtered_tools[i] = {"type": "function", "function": dynamic_schema}
   238→                break
   239→
   240→    if not quiet_mode:
   241→        if filtered_tools:
   242→            tool_names = [t["function"]["name"] for t in filtered_tools]
   243→            print(f"🛠️  Final tool selection ({len(filtered_tools)} tools): {', '.join(tool_names)}")
   244→        else:
   245→            print("🛠️  No tools selected (all filtered out or unavailable)")
   246→
   247→    global _last_resolved_tool_names
   248→    _last_resolved_tool_names = [t["function"]["name"] for t in filtered_tools]
   249→
   250→    return filtered_tools
   251→
   252→
   253→# =============================================================================
   254→# handle_function_call  (the main dispatcher)
   255→# =============================================================================
   256→
   257→# Tools whose execution is intercepted by the agent loop (run_agent.py)
   258→# because they need agent-level state (TodoStore, MemoryStore, etc.).
   259→# The registry still holds their schemas; dispatch just returns a stub error
   260→# so if something slips through, the LLM sees a sensible message.
   261→_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS = {"todo", "memory", "session_search", "delegate_task"}
   262→
   263→
   264→def handle_function_call(
   265→    function_name: str,
   266→    function_args: Dict[str, Any],
   267→    task_id: Optional[str] = None,
   268→    user_task: Optional[str] = None,
   269→    enabled_tools: Optional[List[str]] = None,
   270→) -> str:
   271→    """
   272→    Main function call dispatcher that routes calls to the tool registry.
   273→
   274→    Args:
   275→        function_name: Name of the function to call.
   276→        function_args: Arguments for the function.
   277→        task_id: Unique identifier for terminal/browser session isolation.
   278→        user_task: The user's original task (for browser_snapshot context).
   279→        enabled_tools: Tool names enabled for this session.  When provided,
   280→                       execute_code uses this list to determine which sandbox
   281→                       tools to generate.  Falls back to the process-global
   282→                       ``_last_resolved_tool_names`` for backward compat.
   283→
   284→    Returns:
   285→        Function result as a JSON string.
   286→    """
   287→    # Notify the read-loop tracker when a non-read/search tool runs,
   288→    # so the *consecutive* counter resets (reads after other work are fine).
   289→    _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS = {"read_file", "search_files"}
   290→    if function_name not in _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS:
   291→        try:
   292→            from tools.file_tools import notify_other_tool_call
   293→            notify_other_tool_call(task_id or "default")
   294→        except Exception:
   295→            pass  # file_tools may not be loaded yet
   296→
   297→    try:
   298→        if function_name in _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS:
   299→            return json.dumps({"error": f"{function_name} must be handled by the agent loop"})
   300→
   301→        if function_name == "execute_code":
   302→            # Prefer the caller-provided list so subagents can't overwrite
   303→            # the parent's tool set via the process-global.
   304→            sandbox_enabled = enabled_tools if enabled_tools is not None else _last_resolved_tool_names
   305→            return registry.dispatch(
   306→                function_name, function_args,
   307→                task_id=task_id,
   308→                enabled_tools=sandbox_enabled,
   309→            )
   310→
   311→        return registry.dispatch(
   312→            function_name, function_args,
   313→            task_id=task_id,
   314→            user_task=user_task,
   315→        )
   316→

1→"""Central registry for all hermes-agent tools.
     2→
     3→Each tool file calls ``registry.register()`` at module level to declare its
     4→schema, handler, toolset membership, and availability check.  ``model_tools.py``
     5→queries the registry instead of maintaining its own parallel data structures.
     6→
     7→Import chain (circular-import safe):
     8→    tools/registry.py  (no imports from model_tools or tool files)
     9→           ^
    10→    tools/*.py  (import from tools.registry at module level)
    11→           ^
    12→    model_tools.py  (imports tools.registry + all tool modules)
    13→           ^
    14→    run_agent.py, cli.py, batch_runner.py, etc.
    15→"""
    16→
    17→import json
    18→import logging
    19→from typing import Any, Callable, Dict, List, Optional, Set
    20→
    21→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    22→
    23→
    24→class ToolEntry:
    25→    """Metadata for a single registered tool."""
    26→
    27→    __slots__ = (
    28→        "name", "toolset", "schema", "handler", "check_fn",
    29→        "requires_env", "is_async", "description",
    30→    )
    31→
    32→    def __init__(self, name, toolset, schema, handler, check_fn,
    33→                 requires_env, is_async, description):
    34→        self.name = name
    35→        self.toolset = toolset
    36→        self.schema = schema
    37→        self.handler = handler
    38→        self.check_fn = check_fn
    39→        self.requires_env = requires_env
    40→        self.is_async = is_async
    41→        self.description = description
    42→
    43→
    44→class ToolRegistry:
    45→    """Singleton registry that collects tool schemas + handlers from tool files."""
    46→
    47→    def __init__(self):
    48→        self._tools: Dict[str, ToolEntry] = {}
    49→        self._toolset_checks: Dict[str, Callable] = {}
    50→
    51→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    52→    # Registration
    53→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    54→
    55→    def register(
    56→        self,
    57→        name: str,
    58→        toolset: str,
    59→        schema: dict,
    60→        handler: Callable,
    61→        check_fn: Callable = None,
    62→        requires_env: list = None,
    63→        is_async: bool = False,
    64→        description: str = "",
    65→    ):
    66→        """Register a tool.  Called at module-import time by each tool file."""
    67→        self._tools[name] = ToolEntry(
    68→            name=name,
    69→            toolset=toolset,
    70→            schema=schema,
    71→            handler=handler,
    72→            check_fn=check_fn,
    73→            requires_env=requires_env or [],
    74→            is_async=is_async,
    75→            description=description or schema.get("description", ""),
    76→        )
    77→        if check_fn and toolset not in self._toolset_checks:
    78→            self._toolset_checks[toolset] = check_fn
    79→
    80→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    81→    # Schema retrieval
    82→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    83→
    84→    def get_definitions(self, tool_names: Set[str], quiet: bool = False) -> List[dict]:
    85→        """Return OpenAI-format tool schemas for the requested tool names.
    86→
    87→        Only tools whose ``check_fn()`` returns True (or have no check_fn)
    88→        are included.
    89→        """
    90→        result = []
    91→        for name in sorted(tool_names):
    92→            entry = self._tools.get(name)
    93→            if not entry:
    94→                continue
    95→            if entry.check_fn:
    96→                try:
    97→                    if not entry.check_fn():
    98→                        if not quiet:
    99→                            logger.debug("Tool %s unavailable (check failed)", name)
   100→                        continue
   101→                except Exception:
   102→                    if not quiet:
   103→                        logger.debug("Tool %s check raised; skipping", name)
   104→                    continue
   105→            result.append({"type": "function", "function": entry.schema})
   106→        return result
   107→
   108→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   109→    # Dispatch
   110→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   111→
   112→    def dispatch(self, name: str, args: dict, **kwargs) -> str:
   113→        """Execute a tool handler by name.
   114→
   115→        * Async handlers are bridged automatically via ``_run_async()``.
   116→        * All exceptions are caught and returned as ``{"error": "..."}``
   117→          for consistent error format.
   118→        """
   119→        entry = self._tools.get(name)
   120→        if not entry:
   121→            return json.dumps({"error": f"Unknown tool: {name}"})
   122→        try:
   123→            if entry.is_async:
   124→                from model_tools import _run_async
   125→                return _run_async(entry.handler(args, **kwargs))
   126→            return entry.handler(args, **kwargs)
   127→        except Exception as e:
   128→            logger.exception("Tool %s dispatch error: %s", name, e)
   129→            return json.dumps({"error": f"Tool execution failed: {type(e).__name__}: {e}"})
   130→
   131→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   132→    # Query helpers  (replace redundant dicts in model_tools.py)
   133→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
   134→
   135→    def get_all_tool_names(self) -> List[str]:
   136→        """Return sorted list of all registered tool names."""
   137→        return sorted(self._tools.keys())
   138→
   139→    def get_toolset_for_tool(self, name: str) -> Optional[str]:
   140→        """Return the toolset a tool belongs to, or None."""
   141→        entry = self._tools.get(name)
   142→        return entry.toolset if entry else None
   143→
   144→    def get_tool_to_toolset_map(self) -> Dict[str, str]:
   145→        """Return ``{tool_name: toolset_name}`` for every registered tool."""
   146→        return {name: e.toolset for name, e in self._tools.items()}
   147→

   150→

   157→class IterationBudget:
   158→    """Thread-safe shared iteration counter for parent and child agents.
   159→
   160→    Tracks total LLM-call iterations consumed across a parent agent and all
   161→    its subagents.  A single ``IterationBudget`` is created by the parent
   162→    and passed to every child so they share the same cap.
   163→
   164→    ``execute_code`` (programmatic tool calling) iterations are refunded via
   165→    :meth:`refund` so they don't eat into the budget.
   166→    """
   167→
   168→    def __init__(self, max_total: int):
   169→        self.max_total = max_total
   170→        self._used = 0
   171→        self._lock = threading.Lock()
   172→
   173→    def consume(self) -> bool:
   174→        """Try to consume one iteration.  Returns True if allowed."""
   175→        with self._lock:
   176→            if self._used >= self.max_total:
   177→                return False
   178→            self._used += 1
   179→            return True
   180→
   181→    def refund(self) -> None:
   182→        """Give back one iteration (e.g. for execute_code turns)."""
   183→        with self._lock:
   184→            if self._used > 0:
   185→                self._used -= 1
   186→
   187→    @property
   188→    def used(self) -> int:
   189→        return self._used
   190→
   191→    @property
   192→    def remaining(self) -> int:
   193→        with self._lock:
   194→            return max(0, self.max_total - self._used)
   195→
   196→
   197→# Tools that must never run concurrently (interactive / user-facing).
   198→# When any of these appear in a batch, we fall back to sequential execution.
   199→_NEVER_PARALLEL_TOOLS = frozenset({"clarify"})
   200→
   201→# Maximum number of concurrent worker threads for parallel tool execution.
   202→_MAX_TOOL_WORKERS = 8
