1→"""Shared runtime provider resolution for CLI, gateway, cron, and helpers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→from typing import Any, Dict, Optional
     7→
     8→from hermes_cli.auth import (
     9→    AuthError,
    10→    PROVIDER_REGISTRY,
    11→    format_auth_error,
    12→    resolve_provider,
    13→    resolve_nous_runtime_credentials,
    14→    resolve_codex_runtime_credentials,
    15→    resolve_api_key_provider_credentials,
    16→)
    17→from hermes_cli.config import load_config
    18→from hermes_constants import OPENROUTER_BASE_URL
    19→
    20→
    21→def _get_model_config() -> Dict[str, Any]:
    22→    config = load_config()
    23→    model_cfg = config.get("model")
    24→    if isinstance(model_cfg, dict):
    25→        return dict(model_cfg)
    26→    if isinstance(model_cfg, str) and model_cfg.strip():
    27→        return {"default": model_cfg.strip()}
    28→    return {}
    29→
    30→
    31→def resolve_requested_provider(requested: Optional[str] = None) -> str:
    32→    """Resolve provider request from explicit arg, env, then config."""
    33→    if requested and requested.strip():
    34→        return requested.strip().lower()
    35→
    36→    env_provider = os.getenv("HERMES_INFERENCE_PROVIDER", "").strip().lower()
    37→    if env_provider:
    38→        return env_provider
    39→
    40→    model_cfg = _get_model_config()
    41→    cfg_provider = model_cfg.get("provider")
    42→    if isinstance(cfg_provider, str) and cfg_provider.strip():
    43→        return cfg_provider.strip().lower()
    44→
    45→    return "auto"
    46→
    47→
    48→def _resolve_openrouter_runtime(
    49→    *,
    50→    requested_provider: str,
    51→    explicit_api_key: Optional[str] = None,
    52→    explicit_base_url: Optional[str] = None,
    53→) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    54→    model_cfg = _get_model_config()
    55→    cfg_base_url = model_cfg.get("base_url") if isinstance(model_cfg.get("base_url"), str) else ""
    56→    cfg_provider = model_cfg.get("provider") if isinstance(model_cfg.get("provider"), str) else ""
    57→    requested_norm = (requested_provider or "").strip().lower()
    58→    cfg_provider = cfg_provider.strip().lower()
    59→
    60→    env_openai_base_url = os.getenv("OPENAI_BASE_URL", "").strip()
    61→    env_openrouter_base_url = os.getenv("OPENROUTER_BASE_URL", "").strip()
    62→
    63→    use_config_base_url = False
    64→    if requested_norm == "auto":
    65→        if cfg_base_url.strip() and not explicit_base_url and not env_openai_base_url:
    66→            if not cfg_provider or cfg_provider == "auto":
    67→                use_config_base_url = True
    68→
    69→    # When the user explicitly requested the openrouter provider, skip
    70→    # OPENAI_BASE_URL — it typically points to a custom / non-OpenRouter
    71→    # endpoint and would prevent switching back to OpenRouter (#874).
    72→    skip_openai_base = requested_norm == "openrouter"
    73→
    74→    base_url = (
    75→        (explicit_base_url or "").strip()
    76→        or ("" if skip_openai_base else env_openai_base_url)
    77→        or (cfg_base_url.strip() if use_config_base_url else "")
    78→        or env_openrouter_base_url
    79→        or OPENROUTER_BASE_URL
    80→    ).rstrip("/")
    81→
    82→    # Choose API key based on whether the resolved base_url targets OpenRouter.
    83→    # When hitting OpenRouter, prefer OPENROUTER_API_KEY (issue #289).
    84→    # When hitting a custom endpoint (e.g. Z.ai, local LLM), prefer
    85→    # OPENAI_API_KEY so the OpenRouter key doesn't leak to an unrelated
    86→    # provider (issues #420, #560).
    87→    _is_openrouter_url = "openrouter.ai" in base_url
    88→    if _is_openrouter_url:
    89→        api_key = (
    90→            explicit_api_key
    91→            or os.getenv("OPENROUTER_API_KEY")
    92→            or os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY")
    93→            or ""
    94→        )
    95→    else:
    96→        api_key = (
    97→            explicit_api_key
    98→            or os.getenv("OPENAI_API_KEY")
    99→            or os.getenv("OPENROUTER_API_KEY")
   100→            or ""
   101→        )
   102→
   103→    source = "explicit" if (explicit_api_key or explicit_base_url) else "env/config"
   104→
   105→    return {
   106→        "provider": "openrouter",
   107→        "api_mode": "chat_completions",
   108→        "base_url": base_url,
   109→        "api_key": api_key,
   110→        "source": source,
   111→    }
   112→
   113→
   114→def resolve_runtime_provider(
   115→    *,
   116→    requested: Optional[str] = None,
   117→    explicit_api_key: Optional[str] = None,
   118→    explicit_base_url: Optional[str] = None,
   119→) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   120→    """Resolve runtime provider credentials for agent execution."""
   121→    requested_provider = resolve_requested_provider(requested)
   122→
   123→    provider = resolve_provider(
   124→        requested_provider,
   125→        [REDACTED],
   126→        explicit_base_url=explicit_base_url,
   127→    )
   128→
   129→    if provider == "nous":
   130→        creds = resolve_nous_runtime_credentials(
   131→            min_key_ttl_seconds=max(60, int(os.getenv("HERMES_NOUS_MIN_KEY_TTL_SECONDS", "1800"))),
   132→            timeout_seconds=float(os.getenv("HERMES_NOUS_TIMEOUT_SECONDS", "15")),
   133→        )
   134→        return {
   135→            "provider": "nous",
   136→            "api_mode": "chat_completions",
   137→            "base_url": creds.get("base_url", "").rstrip("/"),
   138→            "api_key": creds.get("api_key", ""),
   139→            "source": creds.get("source", "portal"),
   140→            "expires_at": creds.get("expires_at"),
   141→            "requested_provider": requested_provider,
   142→        }
   143→
   144→    if provider == "openai-codex":
   145→        creds = resolve_codex_runtime_credentials()
   146→        return {
   147→            "provider": "openai-codex",
   148→            "api_mode": "codex_responses",
   149→            "base_url": creds.get("base_url", "").rstrip("/"),
   150→            "api_key": creds.get("api_key", ""),
   151→            "source": creds.get("source", "hermes-auth-store"),
   152→            "last_refresh": creds.get("last_refresh"),
   153→            "requested_provider": requested_provider,
   154→        }
   155→
   156→    # Anthropic (native Messages API)
   157→    if provider == "anthropic":
   158→        from agent.anthropic_adapter import resolve_anthropic_token
   159→        [REDACTED]()
   160→        if not token:
   161→            raise AuthError(
   162→                "No Anthropic credentials found. Set ANTHROPIC_TOKEN or ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, "
   163→                "run 'claude setup-token', or authenticate with 'claude /login'."
   164→            )
   165→        return {
   166→            "provider": "anthropic",
   167→            "api_mode": "anthropic_messages",
   168→            "base_url": "https://api.anthropic.com",
   169→            "api_key": token,
   170→            "source": "env",
   171→            "requested_provider": requested_provider,
   172→        }
   173→
   174→    # API-key providers (z.ai/GLM, Kimi, MiniMax, MiniMax-CN)
   175→    pconfig = PROVIDER_REGISTRY.get(provider)
   176→    if pconfig and pconfig.auth_type == "api_key":
   177→        creds = resolve_api_key_provider_credentials(provider)
   178→        return {
   179→            "provider": provider,
   180→            "api_mode": "chat_completions",
   181→            "base_url": creds.get("base_url", "").rstrip("/"),
   182→            "api_key": creds.get("api_key", ""),
   183→            "source": creds.get("source", "env"),
   184→            "requested_provider": requested_provider,
   185→        }
   186→
   187→    runtime = _resolve_openrouter_runtime(
   188→        requested_provider=requested_provider,
   189→        [REDACTED],
   190→        explicit_base_url=explicit_base_url,
   191→    )
   192→    runtime["requested_provider"] = requested_provider
   193→    return runtime
   194→
   195→
   196→def format_runtime_provider_error(error: Exception) -> str:
   197→    if isinstance(error, AuthError):
   198→        return format_auth_error(error)
   199→    return str(error)
   200→

     5→This module provides a flexible system for defining and managing tool aliases/toolsets.

    10→- Define custom toolsets with specific tools

   280→    }
   281→}
   282→
   283→
   284→
   285→def get_toolset(name: str) -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   286→    """
   287→    Get a toolset definition by name.
   288→    
   289→    Args:
   290→        name (str): Name of the toolset
   291→        
   292→    Returns:
   293→        Dict: Toolset definition with description, tools, and includes
   294→        None: If toolset not found
   295→    """
   296→    # Return toolset definition
   297→    return TOOLSETS.get(name)
   298→
   299→
   300→def resolve_toolset(name: str, visited: Set[str] = None) -> List[str]:
   301→    """
   302→    Recursively resolve a toolset to get all tool names.
   303→    
   304→    This function handles toolset composition by recursively resolving
   305→    included toolsets and combining all tools.
   306→    
   307→    Args:
   308→        name (str): Name of the toolset to resolve
   309→        visited (Set[str]): Set of already visited toolsets (for cycle detection)
   310→        
   311→    Returns:
   312→        List[str]: List of all tool names in the toolset
   313→    """
   314→    if visited is None:
   315→        visited = set()
   316→    
   317→    # Special aliases that represent all tools across every toolset
   318→    # This ensures future toolsets are automatically included without changes.
   319→    if name in {"all", "*"}:
   320→        all_tools: Set[str] = set()
   321→        for toolset_name in get_toolset_names():
   322→            # Use a fresh visited set per branch to avoid cross-branch contamination
   323→            resolved = resolve_toolset(toolset_name, visited.copy())
   324→            all_tools.update(resolved)
   325→        return list(all_tools)
   326→
   327→    # Check for cycles
   328→    if name in visited:
   329→        print(f"⚠️  Circular dependency detected in toolset '{name}'")
   330→        return []
   331→    
   332→    visited.add(name)
   333→    
   334→    # Get toolset definition
   335→    toolset = TOOLSETS.get(name)
   336→    if not toolset:
   337→        return []
   338→    
   339→    # Collect direct tools
   340→    tools = set(toolset.get("tools", []))
   341→    
   342→    # Recursively resolve included toolsets
   343→    for included_name in toolset.get("includes", []):
   344→        included_tools = resolve_toolset(included_name, visited.copy())
   345→        tools.update(included_tools)
   346→    
   347→    return list(tools)
   348→
   349→
   350→def resolve_multiple_toolsets(toolset_names: List[str]) -> List[str]:
   351→    """
   352→    Resolve multiple toolsets and combine their tools.
   353→    
   354→    Args:
   355→        toolset_names (List[str]): List of toolset names to resolve
   356→        
   357→    Returns:
   358→        List[str]: Combined list of all tool names (deduplicated)
   359→    """
   360→    all_tools = set()
   361→    
   362→    for name in toolset_names:
   363→        tools = resolve_toolset(name)
   364→        all_tools.update(tools)
   365→    
   366→    return list(all_tools)
   367→
   368→
   369→def get_all_toolsets() -> Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]]:
   370→    """
   371→    Get all available toolsets with their definitions.
   372→    
   373→    Returns:
   374→        Dict: All toolset definitions
   375→    """
   376→    return TOOLSETS.copy()
   377→
   378→
   379→def get_toolset_names() -> List[str]:
   380→    """
   381→    Get names of all available toolsets (excluding aliases).
   382→    
   383→    Returns:
   384→        List[str]: List of toolset names
   385→    """
   386→    return list(TOOLSETS.keys())
   387→
   388→
   389→
   390→
   391→def validate_toolset(name: str) -> bool:
   392→    """
   393→    Check if a toolset name is valid.
   394→    
   395→    Args:
   396→        name (str): Toolset name to validate
   397→        
   398→    Returns:
   399→        bool: True if valid, False otherwise
   400→    """
   401→    # Accept special alias names for convenience
   402→    if name in {"all", "*"}:
   403→        return True
   404→    return name in TOOLSETS
   405→
   406→
   407→def create_custom_toolset(
   408→    name: str,
   409→    description: str,
   410→    tools: List[str] = None,
   411→    includes: List[str] = None
   412→) -> None:
   413→    """
   414→    Create a custom toolset at runtime.
   415→    
   416→    Args:
   417→        name (str): Name for the new toolset
   418→        description (str): Description of the toolset
   419→        tools (List[str]): Direct tools to include
   420→        includes (List[str]): Other toolsets to include
   421→    """
   422→    TOOLSETS[name] = {
   423→        "description": description,
   424→        "tools": tools or [],
   425→        "includes": includes or []
   426→    }
   427→
   428→
   429→
   430→
   431→def get_toolset_info(name: str) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   432→    """
   433→    Get detailed information about a toolset including resolved tools.
   434→    
   435→    Args:
   436→        name (str): Toolset name
   437→        
   438→    Returns:
   439→        Dict: Detailed toolset information
   440→    """
   441→    toolset = get_toolset(name)
   442→    if not toolset:
   443→        return None
   444→    
   445→    resolved_tools = resolve_toolset(name)
   446→    
   447→    return {
   448→        "name": name,
   449→        "description": toolset["description"],
   450→        "direct_tools": toolset["tools"],
   451→        "includes": toolset["includes"],
   452→        "resolved_tools": resolved_tools,
   453→        "tool_count": len(resolved_tools),
   454→        "is_composite": len(toolset["includes"]) > 0
   455→    }
   456→
   457→
   458→def print_toolset_tree(name: str, indent: int = 0) -> None:
   459→    """
   460→    Print a tree view of a toolset and its composition.
   461→    
   462→    Args:
   463→        name (str): Toolset name
   464→        indent (int): Current indentation level
   465→    """
   466→    prefix = "  " * indent
   467→    toolset = get_toolset(name)
   468→    
   469→    if not toolset:
   470→        print(f"{prefix}❌ Unknown toolset: {name}")
   471→        return
   472→    
   473→    # Print toolset name and description
   474→    print(f"{prefix}📦 {name}: {toolset['description']}")
   475→    
   476→    # Print direct tools
   477→    if toolset["tools"]:
   478→        print(f"{prefix}  🔧 Tools: {', '.join(toolset['tools'])}")
   479→    
   480→    # Print included toolsets
   481→    if toolset["includes"]:
   482→        print(f"{prefix}  📂 Includes:")
   483→        for included in toolset["includes"]:
   484→            print_toolset_tree(included, indent + 2)
   485→
   486→
   487→if __name__ == "__main__":
   488→    print("Toolsets System Demo")
   489→    print("=" * 60)
   490→    
   491→    print("\nAvailable Toolsets:")
   492→    print("-" * 40)
   493→    for name, toolset in get_all_toolsets().items():
   494→        info = get_toolset_info(name)
   495→        composite = "[composite]" if info["is_composite"] else "[leaf]"
   496→        print(f"  {composite} {name:20} - {toolset['description']}")
   497→        print(f"     Tools: {len(info['resolved_tools'])} total")
   498→    
   499→    print("\nToolset Resolution Examples:")
   500→    print("-" * 40)
   501→    for name in ["web", "terminal", "safe", "debugging"]:
   502→        tools = resolve_toolset(name)
   503→        print(f"\n  {name}:")
   504→        print(f"    Resolved to {len(tools)} tools: {', '.join(sorted(tools))}")
   505→    
   506→    print("\nMultiple Toolset Resolution:")
   507→    print("-" * 40)
   508→    combined = resolve_multiple_toolsets(["web", "vision", "terminal"])
   509→    print(f"  Combining ['web', 'vision', 'terminal']:")
   510→    print(f"    Result: {', '.join(sorted(combined))}")
   511→    
   512→    print("\nCustom Toolset Creation:")
   513→    print("-" * 40)
   514→    create_custom_toolset(
   515→        name="my_custom",
   516→        description="My custom toolset for specific tasks",
   517→        tools=["web_search"],
   518→        includes=["terminal", "vision"]
   519→    )
   520→    custom_info = get_toolset_info("my_custom")
   521→    print(f"  Created 'my_custom' toolset:")
   522→    print(f"    Description: {custom_info['description']}")
   523→    print(f"    Resolved tools: {', '.join(custom_info['resolved_tools'])}")
   524→

#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Todo Tool Module - Planning & Task Management

Provides an in-memory task list the agent uses to decompose complex tasks,
track progress, and maintain focus across long conversations. The state
lives on the AIAgent instance (one per session) and is re-injected into
the conversation after context compression events.

Design:
- Single `todo` tool: provide `todos` param to write, omit to read
- Every call returns the full current list
- No system prompt mutation, no tool response modification
- Behavioral guidance lives entirely in the tool schema description
"""

import json
from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional


# Valid status values for todo items
VALID_STATUSES = {"pending", "in_progress", "completed", "cancelled"}


class TodoStore:
    """
    In-memory todo list. One instance per AIAgent (one per session).

    Items are ordered -- list position is priority. Each item has:
      - id: unique string identifier (agent-chosen)
      - content: task description
      - status: pending | in_progress | completed | cancelled
    """

    def __init__(self):
        self._items: List[Dict[str, str]] = []

    def write(self, todos: List[Dict[str, Any]], merge: bool = False) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
        """
        Write todos. Returns the full current list after writing.

        Args:
            todos: list of {id, content, status} dicts
            merge: if False, replace the entire list. If True, update
                   existing items by id and append new ones.
        """
        if not merge:
            # Replace mode: new list entirely
            self._items = [self._validate(t) for t in todos]
        else:
            # Merge mode: update existing items by id, append new ones
            existing = {item["id"]: item for item in self._items}
            for t in todos:
                item_id = str(t.get("id", "")).strip()
                if not item_id:
                    continue  # Can't merge without an id

                if item_id in existing:
                    # Update only the fields the LLM actually provided
                    if "content" in t and t["content"]:
                        existing[item_id]["content"] = str(t["content"]).strip()
                    if "status" in t and t["status"]:
                        status = str(t["status"]).strip().lower()
                        if status in VALID_STATUSES:
                            existing[item_id]["status"] = status
                else:
                    # New item -- validate fully and append to end
                    validated = self._validate(t)
                    existing[validated["id"]] = validated
                    self._items.append(validated)
            # Rebuild _items preserving order for existing items
            seen = set()
            rebuilt = []
            for item in self._items:
                current = existing.get(item["id"], item)
                if current["id"] not in seen:
                    rebuilt.append(current)
                    seen.add(current["id"])
            self._items = rebuilt
        return self.read()

    def read(self) -> List[Dict[str, str]]:
        """Return a copy of the current list."""
        return [item.copy() for item in self._items]

    def has_items(self) -> bool:
        """Check if there are any items in the list."""
        return len(self._items) > 0

    def format_for_injection(self) -> Optional[str]:
        """
        Render the todo list for post-compression injection.

        Returns a human-readable string to append to the compressed
        message history, or None if the list is empty.
        """
        if not self._items:
            return None

        # Status markers for compact display
```

1→"""Shared curses-based UI components for Hermes CLI.
     2→
     3→Used by `hermes tools` and `hermes skills` for interactive checklists.
     4→Provides a curses multi-select with keyboard navigation, plus a
     5→text-based numbered fallback for terminals without curses support.
     6→"""
     7→from typing import List, Set
     8→
     9→from hermes_cli.colors import Colors, color
    10→
    11→
    12→def curses_checklist(
    13→    title: str,
    14→    items: List[str],
    15→    selected: Set[int],
    16→    *,
    17→    cancel_returns: Set[int] | None = None,
    18→) -> Set[int]:
    19→    """Curses multi-select checklist. Returns set of selected indices.
    20→
    21→    Args:
    22→        title: Header line displayed above the checklist.
    23→        items: Display labels for each row.
    24→        selected: Indices that start checked (pre-selected).
    25→        cancel_returns: Returned on ESC/q. Defaults to the original *selected*.
    26→    """
    27→    if cancel_returns is None:
    28→        cancel_returns = set(selected)
    29→
    30→    try:
    31→        import curses
    32→        chosen = set(selected)
    33→        result_holder: list = [None]
    34→
    35→        def _draw(stdscr):
    36→            curses.curs_set(0)
    37→            if curses.has_colors():
    38→                curses.start_color()
    39→                curses.use_default_colors()
    40→                curses.init_pair(1, curses.COLOR_GREEN, -1)
    41→                curses.init_pair(2, curses.COLOR_YELLOW, -1)
    42→                curses.init_pair(3, 8, -1)  # dim gray
    43→            cursor = 0
    44→            scroll_offset = 0
    45→
    46→            while True:
    47→                stdscr.clear()
    48→                max_y, max_x = stdscr.getmaxyx()
    49→
    50→                # Header
    51→                try:
    52→                    hattr = curses.A_BOLD
    53→                    if curses.has_colors():
    54→                        hattr |= curses.color_pair(2)
    55→                    stdscr.addnstr(0, 0, title, max_x - 1, hattr)
    56→                    stdscr.addnstr(
    57→                        1, 0,
    58→                        "  ↑↓ navigate  SPACE toggle  ENTER confirm  ESC cancel",
    59→                        max_x - 1, curses.A_DIM,
    60→                    )
    61→                except curses.error:
    62→                    pass
    63→
    64→                # Scrollable item list
    65→                visible_rows = max_y - 3
    66→                if cursor < scroll_offset:
    67→                    scroll_offset = cursor
    68→                elif cursor >= scroll_offset + visible_rows:
    69→                    scroll_offset = cursor - visible_rows + 1
    70→
    71→                for draw_i, i in enumerate(
    72→                    range(scroll_offset, min(len(items), scroll_offset + visible_rows))
    73→                ):
    74→                    y = draw_i + 3
    75→                    if y >= max_y - 1:
    76→                        break
    77→                    check = "✓" if i in chosen else " "
    78→                    arrow = "→" if i == cursor else " "
    79→                    line = f" {arrow} [{check}] {items[i]}"
    80→                    attr = curses.A_NORMAL
    81→                    if i == cursor:
    82→                        attr = curses.A_BOLD
    83→                        if curses.has_colors():
    84→                            attr |= curses.color_pair(1)
    85→                    try:

1→"""Interactive prompt callbacks for terminal_tool integration.
     2→
     3→These bridge terminal_tool's interactive prompts (clarify, sudo, approval)
     4→into prompt_toolkit's event loop. Each function takes the HermesCLI instance
     5→as its first argument and uses its state (queues, app reference) to coordinate
     6→with the TUI.
     7→"""
     8→
     9→import queue
    10→import time as _time
    11→import getpass
    12→
    13→from hermes_cli.banner import cprint, _DIM, _RST
    14→from hermes_cli.config import save_env_value_secure
    15→
    16→
    17→def clarify_callback(cli, question, choices):
    18→    """Prompt for clarifying question through the TUI.
    19→
    20→    Sets up the interactive selection UI, then blocks until the user
    21→    responds. Returns the user's choice or a timeout message.
    22→    """
    23→    from cli import CLI_CONFIG
    24→
    25→    timeout = CLI_CONFIG.get("clarify", {}).get("timeout", 120)
    26→    response_queue = queue.Queue()
    27→    is_open_ended = not choices or len(choices) == 0
    28→
    29→    cli._clarify_state = {
    30→        "question": question,
    31→        "choices": choices if not is_open_ended else [],
    32→        "selected": 0,
    33→        "response_queue": response_queue,
    34→    }
    35→    cli._clarify_deadline = _time.monotonic() + timeout
    36→    cli._clarify_freetext = is_open_ended
    37→
    38→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    39→        cli._app.invalidate()
    40→
    41→    while True:
    42→        try:
    43→            result = response_queue.get(timeout=1)
    44→            cli._clarify_deadline = 0
    45→            return result
    46→        except queue.Empty:
    47→            remaining = cli._clarify_deadline - _time.monotonic()
    48→            if remaining <= 0:
    49→                break
    50→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    51→                cli._app.invalidate()
    52→
    53→    cli._clarify_state = None
    54→    cli._clarify_freetext = False
    55→    cli._clarify_deadline = 0
    56→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    57→        cli._app.invalidate()
    58→    cprint(f"\n{_DIM}(clarify timed out after {timeout}s — agent will decide){_RST}")
    59→    return (
    60→        "The user did not provide a response within the time limit. "
    61→        "Use your best judgement to make the choice and proceed."
    62→    )
    63→
    64→
    65→def sudo_[REDACTED](cli) -> str:
    66→    """Prompt for sudo password through the TUI.
    67→
    68→    Sets up a password input area and blocks until the user responds.
    69→    """
    70→    timeout = 45
    71→    response_queue = queue.Queue()
    72→
    73→    cli._sudo_state = {"response_queue": response_queue}
    74→    cli._sudo_deadline = _time.monotonic() + timeout
    75→
    76→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    77→        cli._app.invalidate()
    78→
    79→    while True:
    80→        try:
    81→            result = response_queue.get(timeout=1)
    82→            cli._sudo_state = None
    83→            cli._sudo_deadline = 0
    84→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    85→                cli._app.invalidate()
    86→            if result:
    87→                cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ✓ [REDACTED] (cached for session){_RST}")
    88→            else:
    89→                cprint(f"\n{_DIM}  ⏭ Skipped{_RST}")
    90→            return result
    91→        except queue.Empty:
    92→            remaining = cli._sudo_deadline - _time.monotonic()
    93→            if remaining <= 0:
    94→                break
    95→            if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:
    96→                cli._app.invalidate()
    97→
    98→    cli._sudo_state = None
    99→    cli._sudo_deadline = 0
   100→    if hasattr(cli, "_app") and cli._app:

     2→"""
     3→Hermes CLI - Main entry point.
     4→
     5→Usage:
     6→    hermes                     # Interactive chat (default)
     7→    hermes chat                # Interactive chat
     8→    hermes gateway             # Run gateway in foreground
     9→    hermes gateway start       # Start gateway as service
    10→    hermes gateway stop        # Stop gateway service
    11→    hermes gateway status      # Show gateway status
    12→    hermes gateway install     # Install gateway service
    13→    hermes gateway uninstall   # Uninstall gateway service
    14→    hermes setup               # Interactive setup wizard
    15→    hermes logout              # Clear stored authentication
    16→    hermes status              # Show status of all components
    17→    hermes cron                # Manage cron jobs
    18→    hermes cron list           # List cron jobs
    19→    hermes cron status         # Check if cron scheduler is running
    20→    hermes doctor              # Check configuration and dependencies
    21→    hermes honcho setup                    # Configure Honcho AI memory integration
    22→    hermes honcho status                   # Show Honcho config and connection status
    23→    hermes honcho sessions                 # List directory → session name mappings
    24→    hermes honcho map <name>               # Map current directory to a session name
    25→    hermes honcho peer                     # Show peer names and dialectic settings
    26→    hermes honcho peer --user NAME         # Set user peer name
    27→    hermes honcho peer --ai NAME           # Set AI peer name
    28→    hermes honcho peer --reasoning LEVEL   # Set dialectic reasoning level
    29→    hermes honcho mode                     # Show current memory mode
    30→    hermes honcho mode [hybrid|honcho|local]  # Set memory mode
    31→    hermes honcho tokens                   # Show token budget settings
    32→    hermes honcho tokens --context N       # Set session.context() token cap
    33→    hermes honcho tokens --dialectic N     # Set dialectic result char cap
    34→    hermes honcho identity                 # Show AI peer identity representation

    37→    hermes version             # Show version

    40→    hermes sessions browse     # Interactive session picker with search
    41→    hermes claw migrate        # Migrate from OpenClaw to Hermes
    42→    hermes claw migrate --dry-run  # Preview migration without changes

   139→        inference_base_url="https://api.anthropic.com",
   140→        api_key_env_vars=("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", "ANTHROPIC_TOKEN", "CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN"),
   141→    ),
   142→    "minimax-cn": ProviderConfig(
   143→        id="minimax-cn",
   144→        name="MiniMax (China)",
   145→        auth_type="api_key",
   146→        inference_base_url="https://api.minimaxi.com/v1",
   147→        api_key_env_vars=("MINIMAX_CN_API_KEY",),

   149→    ),
   150→}
   151→
   152→
   153→# =============================================================================
   154→# Kimi Code Endpoint Detection
   155→# =============================================================================
   156→
   157→# Kimi Code (platform.kimi.ai) issues keys prefixed "sk-kimi-" that only work
   158→# on api.kimi.com/coding/v1.  Legacy keys from platform.moonshot.ai work on
   159→# api.moonshot.ai/v1 (the default).  Auto-detect when user hasn't set
   160→# KIMI_BASE_URL explicitly.
   161→KIMI_CODE_BASE_URL = "https://api.kimi.com/coding/v1"
   162→
   163→
   164→def _resolve_kimi_base_url(api_key: str, default_url: str, env_override: str) -> str:
   165→    """Return the correct Kimi base URL based on the API key prefix.
   166→
   167→    If the user has explicitly set KIMI_BASE_URL, that always wins.
   168→    Otherwise, sk-kimi- prefixed keys route to api.kimi.com/coding/v1.
   169→    """
   170→    if env_override:
   171→        return env_override
   172→    if api_key.startswith("sk-kimi-"):
   173→        return KIMI_CODE_BASE_URL
   174→    return default_url
   175→
   176→
   177→# =============================================================================
   178→# Z.AI Endpoint Detection
   179→# =============================================================================
   180→
   181→# Z.AI has separate billing for general vs coding plans, and global vs China
   182→# endpoints.  A key that works on one may return "Insufficient balance" on
   183→# another.  We probe at setup time and store the working endpoint.
   184→
   185→ZAI_ENDPOINTS = [
   186→    # (id, base_url, default_model, label)
   187→    ("global",        "https://api.z.ai/api/paas/v4",        "glm-5",   "Global"),
   188→    ("cn",            "https://open.bigmodel.cn/api/paas/v4", "glm-5",   "China"),
   189→    ("coding-global", "https://api.z.ai/api/coding/paas/v4",  "glm-4.7", "Global (Coding Plan)"),
   190→    ("coding-cn",     "https://open.bigmodel.cn/api/coding/paas/v4", "glm-4.7", "China (Coding Plan)"),
   191→]
   192→
   193→
   194→def detect_zai_endpoint(api_key: str, timeout: float = 8.0) -> Optional[Dict[str, str]]:
   195→    """Probe z.ai endpoints to find one that accepts this API key.
   196→
   197→    Returns {"id": ..., "base_url": ..., "model": ..., "label": ...} for the
   198→    first working endpoint, or None if all fail.
   199→    """
   200→    for ep_id, base_url, model, label in ZAI_ENDPOINTS:
   201→        try:
   202→            resp = httpx.post(
   203→                f"{base_url}/chat/completions",
   204→                headers={
   205→                    "Authorization": f"Bearer {api_key}",
   206→                    "Content-Type": "application/json",
   207→                },
   208→                json={
   209→                    "model": model,
   210→                    "stream": False,
   211→                    "max_tokens": 1,
   212→                    "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": "ping"}],
   213→                },
   214→                timeout=timeout,
   215→            )
   216→            if resp.status_code == 200:
   217→                logger.debug("Z.AI endpoint probe: %s (%s) OK", ep_id, base_url)
   218→                return {
   219→                    "id": ep_id,
   220→                    "base_url": base_url,
   221→                    "model": model,
   222→                    "label": label,
   223→                }
   224→            logger.debug("Z.AI endpoint probe: %s returned %s", ep_id, resp.status_code)
   225→        except Exception as exc:
   226→            logger.debug("Z.AI endpoint probe: %s failed: %s", ep_id, exc)
   227→    return None
   228→

1→"""Anthropic Messages API adapter for Hermes Agent.
     2→
     3→Translates between Hermes's internal OpenAI-style message format and
     4→Anthropic's Messages API. Follows the same pattern as the codex_responses
     5→adapter — all provider-specific logic is isolated here.
     6→
     7→Auth supports:
     8→  - Regular API keys (sk-ant-api*) → x-api-key header
     9→  - OAuth setup-tokens (sk-ant-oat*) → Bearer auth + beta header
    10→  - Claude Code credentials (~/.claude.json or ~/.claude/.credentials.json) → Bearer auth
    11→"""
    12→
    13→import json
    14→import logging
    15→import os
    16→from pathlib import Path
    17→from types import SimpleNamespace
    18→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
    19→
    20→try:
    21→    import anthropic as _anthropic_sdk
    22→except ImportError:
    23→    _anthropic_sdk = None  # type: ignore[assignment]
    24→
    25→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    26→
    27→THINKING_BUDGET = {"xhigh": 32000, "high": 16000, "medium": 8000, "low": 4000}
    28→ADAPTIVE_EFFORT_MAP = {
    29→    "xhigh": "max",
    30→    "high": "high",
    31→    "medium": "medium",
    32→    "low": "low",
    33→    "minimal": "low",
    34→}
    35→
    36→
    37→def _supports_adaptive_thinking(model: str) -> bool:
    38→    """Return True for Claude 4.6 models that support adaptive thinking."""
    39→    return any(v in model for v in ("4-6", "4.6"))
    40→
    41→
    42→# Beta headers for enhanced features (sent with ALL auth types)
    43→_COMMON_BETAS = [
    44→    "interleaved-thinking-2025-05-14",
    45→    "fine-grained-tool-streaming-2025-05-14",
    46→]
    47→
    48→# Additional beta headers required for OAuth/subscription auth
    49→# Both clawdbot and OpenCode include claude-code-20250219 alongside oauth-2025-04-20.
    50→# Without claude-code-20250219, Anthropic's API rejects OAuth tokens with 401.
    51→_OAUTH_ONLY_BETAS = [
    52→    "claude-code-20250219",
    53→    "oauth-2025-04-20",
    54→]
    55→
    56→
    57→def _is_oauth_token(key: str) -> bool:
    58→    """Check if the key is an OAuth/setup token (not a regular Console API key).
    59→
    60→    Regular API keys start with 'sk-ant-api'. Everything else (setup-tokens
    61→    starting with 'sk-ant-oat', managed keys, JWTs, etc.) needs Bearer auth.
    62→    """
    63→    if not key:
    64→        return False
    65→    # Regular Console API keys use x-api-key header
    66→    if key.startswith("sk-ant-api"):
    67→        return False
    68→    # Everything else (setup-tokens, managed keys, JWTs) uses Bearer auth
    69→    return True
    70→
    71→
    72→def build_anthropic_client(api_key: str, base_url: str = None):
    73→    """Create an Anthropic client, auto-detecting setup-tokens vs API keys.
    74→
    75→    Returns an anthropic.Anthropic instance.
    76→    """
    77→    if _anthropic_sdk is None:
    78→        raise ImportError(
    79→            "The 'anthropic' package is required for the Anthropic provider. "
    80→            "Install it with: pip install 'anthropic>=0.39.0'"
    81→        )
    82→    from httpx import Timeout
    83→
    84→    kwargs = {
    85→        "timeout": Timeout(timeout=900.0, connect=10.0),
    86→    }
    87→    if base_url:
    88→        kwargs["base_url"] = base_url
    89→
    90→    if _is_oauth_token(api_key):
    91→        # OAuth access token / setup-token → Bearer auth + beta headers
    92→        all_betas = _COMMON_BETAS + _OAUTH_ONLY_BETAS
    93→        kwargs["auth_token"] = api_key
    94→        kwargs["default_headers"] = {"anthropic-beta": ",".join(all_betas)}
    95→    else:
    96→        # Regular API key → x-api-key header + common betas
    97→        kwargs["api_key"] = api_key
    98→        if _COMMON_BETAS:
    99→            kwargs["default_headers"] = {"anthropic-beta": ",".join(_COMMON_BETAS)}
   100→
   101→    return _anthropic_sdk.Anthropic(**kwargs)
   102→
   103→
   104→def read_claude_code_credentials() -> Optional[Dict[str, Any]]:
   105→    """Read credentials from Claude Code's config files.
   106→
   107→    Checks two locations (in order):
   108→      1. ~/.claude.json — top-level primaryApiKey (native binary, v2.x)
   109→      2. ~/.claude/.credentials.json — claudeAiOauth block (npm/legacy installs)
   110→
   111→    Returns dict with {accessToken, refreshToken?, expiresAt?} or None.
   112→    """
   113→    # 1. Native binary (v2.x): ~/.claude.json with top-level primaryApiKey
   114→    claude_json = Path.home() / ".claude.json"
   115→    if claude_json.exists():
   116→        try:
   117→            data = json.loads(claude_json.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   118→            primary_key = data.get("primaryApiKey", "")
   119→            if primary_key:
   120→                return {
   121→                    "accessToken": primary_key,
   122→                    "refreshToken": "",
   123→                    "expiresAt": 0,  # Managed keys don't have a user-visible expiry
   124→                }
   125→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError, IOError) as e:
   126→            logger.debug("Failed to read ~/.claude.json: %s", e)
   127→
   128→    # 2. Legacy/npm installs: ~/.claude/.credentials.json
   129→    cred_path = Path.home() / ".claude" / ".credentials.json"
   130→    if cred_path.exists():
   131→        try:
   132→            data = json.loads(cred_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   133→            oauth_data = data.get("claudeAiOauth")
   134→            if oauth_data and isinstance(oauth_data, dict):
   135→                access_token = oauth_data.get("accessToken", "")
   136→                if access_token:
   137→                    return {
   138→                        "accessToken": access_token,
   139→                        "refreshToken": oauth_data.get("refreshToken", ""),
   140→                        "expiresAt": oauth_data.get("expiresAt", 0),
   141→                    }
   142→        except (json.JSONDecodeError, OSError, IOError) as e:
   143→            logger.debug("Failed to read ~/.claude/.credentials.json: %s", e)
   144→
   145→    return None
   146→
   147→
   148→def is_claude_code_token_valid(creds: Dict[str, Any]) -> bool:
   149→    """Check if Claude Code credentials have a non-expired access token."""
   150→    import time
   151→
   152→    expires_at = creds.get("expiresAt", 0)
   153→    if not expires_at:
   154→        # No expiry set (managed keys) — valid if token is present
   155→        return bool(creds.get("accessToken"))
   156→
   157→    # expiresAt is in milliseconds since epoch
   158→    now_ms = int(time.time() * 1000)
   159→    # Allow 60 seconds of buffer
   160→    return now_ms < (expires_at - 60_000)
   161→
   162→
   163→def _refresh_oauth_token(creds: Dict[str, Any]) -> Optional[str]:
   164→    """Attempt to refresh an expired Claude Code OAuth token.
   165→
   166→    Uses the same token endpoint and client_id as Claude Code / OpenCode.
   167→    Only works for credentials that have a refresh token (from claude /login
   168→    or claude setup-token with OAuth flow).
   169→
   170→    Returns the new access token, or None if refresh fails.
   171→    """
   172→    import urllib.parse
   173→    import urllib.request
   174→
   175→    refresh_token = creds.get("refreshToken", "")
   176→    if not refresh_token:
   177→        logger.debug("No refresh token available — cannot refresh")
   178→        return None
   179→
   180→    # Client ID used by Claude Code's OAuth flow
   181→    CLIENT_ID = "9d1c250a-e61b-44d9-88ed-5944d1962f5e"
   182→
   183→    data = urllib.parse.urlencode({
   184→        "grant_type": "refresh_token",
   185→        "refresh_token": refresh_token,
   186→        "client_id": CLIENT_ID,
   187→    }).encode()
   188→
   189→    req = urllib.request.Request(
   190→        "https://console.anthropic.com/v1/oauth/token",
   191→        data=data,
   192→        headers={"Content-Type": "application/x-www-form-urlencoded"},
   193→        method="POST",
   194→    )
   195→
   196→    try:
   197→        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=10) as resp:
   198→            result = json.loads(resp.read().decode())
   199→            new_access = result.get("access_token", "")
   200→            new_refresh = result.get("refresh_token", refresh_token)
   201→            expires_in = result.get("expires_in", 3600)  # seconds
   202→
   203→            if new_access:
   204→                import time
   205→                new_expires_ms = int(time.time() * 1000) + (expires_in * 1000)
   206→                # Write refreshed credentials back to ~/.claude/.credentials.json
   207→                _write_claude_code_credentials(new_access, new_refresh, new_expires_ms)
   208→                logger.debug("Successfully refreshed Claude Code OAuth token")
   209→                return new_access
   210→    except Exception as e:
   211→        logger.debug("Failed to refresh Claude Code token: %s", e)
   212→
   213→    return None
   214→
   215→
   216→def _write_claude_code_credentials(access_token: str, refresh_token: str, expires_at_ms: int) -> None:
   217→    """Write refreshed credentials back to ~/.claude/.credentials.json."""
   218→    cred_path = Path.home() / ".claude" / ".credentials.json"
   219→    try:
   220→        # Read existing file to preserve other fields
   221→        existing = {}
   222→        if cred_path.exists():
   223→            existing = json.loads(cred_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
   224→
   225→        existing["claudeAiOauth"] = {
   226→            "accessToken": access_token,
   227→            "refreshToken": refresh_token,
   228→            "expiresAt": expires_at_ms,
   229→        }
   230→
   231→        cred_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   232→        cred_path.write_text(json.dumps(existing, indent=2), encoding="utf-8")
   233→        # Restrict permissions (credentials file)
   234→        cred_path.chmod(0o600)
   235→    except (OSError, IOError) as e:
   236→        logger.debug("Failed to write refreshed credentials: %s", e)
   237→
   238→
   239→def resolve_anthropic_token() -> Optional[str]:
   240→    """Resolve an Anthropic token from all available sources.
   241→
   242→    Priority:
   243→      1. ANTHROPIC_TOKEN env var (OAuth/setup token saved by Hermes)
   244→      2. CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN env var
   245→      3. Claude Code credentials (~/.claude.json or ~/.claude/.credentials.json)
   246→         — with automatic refresh if expired and a refresh token is available
   247→      4. ANTHROPIC_API_KEY env var (regular API key, or legacy fallback)
   248→
   249→    Returns the token string or None.
   250→    """
   251→    # 1. Hermes-managed OAuth/setup token env var
   252→    token = os.getenv("ANTHROPIC_TOKEN", "").strip()
   253→    if token:
   254→        return token
   255→
   256→    # 2. CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN (used by Claude Code for setup-tokens)
   257→    cc_token = os.getenv("CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN", "").strip()
   258→    if cc_token:
   259→        return cc_token
   260→
   261→    # 3. Claude Code credential file
   262→    creds = read_claude_code_credentials()
   263→    if creds and is_claude_code_token_valid(creds):
   264→        logger.debug("Using Claude Code credentials (auto-detected)")
   265→        return creds["accessToken"]
   266→    elif creds:
   267→        # Token expired — attempt to refresh
   268→        logger.debug("Claude Code credentials expired — attempting refresh")
   269→        refreshed = _refresh_oauth_token(creds)
   270→        if refreshed:
   271→            return refreshed
   272→        logger.debug("Token refresh failed — re-run 'claude setup-token' to reauthenticate")
   273→
   274→    # 4. Regular API key, or a legacy OAuth token saved in ANTHROPIC_API_KEY.
   275→    # This remains as a compatibility fallback for pre-migration Hermes configs.
   276→    api_key = os.getenv("ANTHROPIC_API_KEY", "").strip()
   277→    if api_key:
   278→        return api_key
   279→
   280→    return None
   281→
   282→
   283→def run_oauth_setup_token() -> Optional[str]:
   284→    """Run 'claude setup-token' interactively and return the resulting token.
   285→
   286→    Checks multiple sources after the subprocess completes:
   287→      1. Claude Code credential files (may be written by the subprocess)
   288→      2. CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN / ANTHROPIC_TOKEN env vars
   289→
   290→    Returns the token string, or None if no credentials were obtained.
   291→    Raises FileNotFoundError if the 'claude' CLI is not installed.
   292→    """
   293→    import shutil
   294→    import subprocess
   295→
   296→    claude_path = shutil.which("claude")
   297→    if not claude_path:
   298→        raise FileNotFoundError(
   299→            "The 'claude' CLI is not installed. "
   300→            "Install it with: npm install -g @anthropic-ai/claude-code"
   301→        )
   302→
   303→    # Run interactively — stdin/stdout/stderr inherited so user can interact
   304→    try:
   305→        subprocess.run([claude_path, "setup-token"])
   306→    except (KeyboardInterrupt, EOFError):
   307→        return None
   308→
   309→    # Check if credentials were saved to Claude Code's config files
   310→    creds = read_claude_code_credentials()
   311→    if creds and is_claude_code_token_valid(creds):
   312→        return creds["accessToken"]
   313→
   314→    # Check env vars that may have been set
   315→    for env_var in ("CLAUDE_CODE_OAUTH_TOKEN", "ANTHROPIC_TOKEN"):
   316→        val = os.getenv(env_var, "").strip()
   317→        if val:
   318→            return val
   319→
   320→    return None
   321→
   322→
   323→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   324→# Message / tool / response format conversion
   325→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   326→
   327→
   328→def normalize_model_name(model: str) -> str:
   329→    """Normalize a model name for the Anthropic API.
   330→
   331→    - Strips 'anthropic/' prefix (OpenRouter format, case-insensitive)
   332→    - Converts dots to hyphens in version numbers (OpenRouter uses dots,
   333→      Anthropic uses hyphens: claude-opus-4.6 → claude-opus-4-6)
   334→    """
   335→    lower = model.lower()
   336→    if lower.startswith("anthropic/"):
   337→        model = model[len("anthropic/"):]
   338→    # OpenRouter uses dots for version separators (claude-opus-4.6),
   339→    # Anthropic uses hyphens (claude-opus-4-6). Convert dots to hyphens.
   340→    model = model.replace(".", "-")
   341→    return model
   342→
   343→
   344→def _sanitize_tool_id(tool_id: str) -> str:
   345→    """Sanitize a tool call ID for the Anthropic API.
   346→
   347→    Anthropic requires IDs matching [a-zA-Z0-9_-]. Replace invalid
   348→    characters with underscores and ensure non-empty.
   349→    """
   350→    import re
   351→    if not tool_id:
   352→        return "tool_0"
   353→    sanitized = re.sub(r"[^a-zA-Z0-9_-]", "_", tool_id)
   354→    return sanitized or "tool_0"
   355→
   356→
   357→def convert_tools_to_anthropic(tools: List[Dict]) -> List[Dict]:
   358→    """Convert OpenAI tool definitions to Anthropic format."""
   359→    if not tools:
   360→        return []
   361→    result = []
   362→    for t in tools:
   363→        fn = t.get("function", {})
   364→        result.append({
   365→            "name": fn.get("name", ""),
   366→            "description": fn.get("description", ""),
   367→            "input_schema": fn.get("parameters", {"type": "object", "properties": {}}),
   368→        })
   369→    return result
   370→
   371→
   372→def convert_messages_to_anthropic(
   373→    messages: List[Dict],
   374→) -> Tuple[Optional[Any], List[Dict]]:
   375→    """Convert OpenAI-format messages to Anthropic format.
   376→
   377→    Returns (system_prompt, anthropic_messages).
   378→    System messages are extracted since Anthropic takes them as a separate param.
   379→    system_prompt is a string or list of content blocks (when cache_control present).
   380→    """
   381→    system = None
   382→    result = []
   383→
   384→    for m in messages:
   385→        role = m.get("role", "user")
   386→        content = m.get("content", "")
   387→
   388→        if role == "system":
   389→            if isinstance(content, list):
   390→                # Preserve cache_control markers on content blocks
   391→                has_cache = any(
   392→                    p.get("cache_control") for p in content if isinstance(p, dict)
   393→                )
   394→                if has_cache:
   395→                    system = [p for p in content if isinstance(p, dict)]
   396→                else:
   397→                    system = "\n".join(
   398→                        p["text"] for p in content if p.get("type") == "text"
   399→                    )
   400→            else:
   401→                system = content
   402→            continue
   403→
   404→        if role == "assistant":
   405→            blocks = []
   406→            if content:
   407→                text = content if isinstance(content, str) else json.dumps(content)
   408→                blocks.append({"type": "text", "text": text})
   409→            for tc in m.get("tool_calls", []):
   410→                fn = tc.get("function", {})
   411→                args = fn.get("arguments", "{}")
   412→                try:
   413→                    parsed_args = json.loads(args) if isinstance(args, str) else args
   414→                except (json.JSONDecodeError, ValueError):
   415→                    parsed_args = {}
   416→                blocks.append({
   417→                    "type": "tool_use",
   418→                    "id": _sanitize_tool_id(tc.get("id", "")),
   419→                    "name": fn.get("name", ""),
   420→                    "input": parsed_args,
   421→                })
   422→            # Anthropic rejects empty assistant content
   423→            effective = blocks or content
   424→            if not effective or effective == "":
   425→                effective = [{"type": "text", "text": "(empty)"}]
   426→            result.append({"role": "assistant", "content": effective})
   427→            continue
   428→
   429→        if role == "tool":
   430→            # Sanitize tool_use_id and ensure non-empty content
   431→            result_content = content if isinstance(content, str) else json.dumps(content)
   432→            if not result_content:
   433→                result_content = "(no output)"
   434→            tool_result = {
   435→                "type": "tool_result",
   436→                "tool_use_id": _sanitize_tool_id(m.get("tool_call_id", "")),
   437→                "content": result_content,
   438→            }
   439→            # Merge consecutive tool results into one user message
   440→            if (
   441→                result
   442→                and result[-1]["role"] == "user"
   443→                and isinstance(result[-1]["content"], list)
   444→                and result[-1]["content"]
   445→                and result[-1]["content"][0].get("type") == "tool_result"
   446→            ):
   447→                result[-1]["content"].append(tool_result)
   448→            else:
   449→                result.append({"role": "user", "content": [tool_result]})
   450→            continue
   451→
   452→        # Regular user message
   453→        result.append({"role": "user", "content": content})
   454→
   455→    # Strip orphaned tool_use blocks (no matching tool_result follows)
   456→    tool_result_ids = set()
   457→    for m in result:
   458→        if m["role"] == "user" and isinstance(m["content"], list):
   459→            for block in m["content"]:
   460→                if block.get("type") == "tool_result":
   461→                    tool_result_ids.add(block.get("tool_use_id"))
   462→    for m in result:
   463→        if m["role"] == "assistant" and isinstance(m["content"], list):
   464→            m["content"] = [
   465→                b
   466→                for b in m["content"]
   467→                if b.get("type") != "tool_use" or b.get("id") in tool_result_ids
   468→            ]
   469→            if not m["content"]:
   470→                m["content"] = [{"type": "text", "text": "(tool call removed)"}]
   471→
   472→    # Enforce strict role alternation (Anthropic rejects consecutive same-role messages)
   473→    fixed = []
   474→    for m in result:
   475→        if fixed and fixed[-1]["role"] == m["role"]:
   476→            if m["role"] == "user":
   477→                # Merge consecutive user messages
   478→                prev_content = fixed[-1]["content"]
   479→                curr_content = m["content"]
   480→                if isinstance(prev_content, str) and isinstance(curr_content, str):
   481→                    fixed[-1]["content"] = prev_content + "\n" + curr_content
   482→                elif isinstance(prev_content, list) and isinstance(curr_content, list):
   483→                    fixed[-1]["content"] = prev_content + curr_content
   484→                else:
   485→                    # Mixed types — wrap string in list
   486→                    if isinstance(prev_content, str):
   487→                        prev_content = [{"type": "text", "text": prev_content}]
   488→                    if isinstance(curr_content, str):
   489→                        curr_content = [{"type": "text", "text": curr_content}]
   490→                    fixed[-1]["content"] = prev_content + curr_content
   491→            else:
   492→                # Consecutive assistant messages — merge text content
   493→                prev_blocks = fixed[-1]["content"]
   494→                curr_blocks = m["content"]
   495→                if isinstance(prev_blocks, list) and isinstance(curr_blocks, list):
   496→                    fixed[-1]["content"] = prev_blocks + curr_blocks
   497→                elif isinstance(prev_blocks, str) and isinstance(curr_blocks, str):
   498→                    fixed[-1]["content"] = prev_blocks + "\n" + curr_blocks
   499→                else:
   500→                    # Keep the later message
   501→                    fixed[-1] = m
   502→        else:
   503→            fixed.append(m)
   504→    result = fixed
   505→
   506→    return system, result
   507→
   508→
   509→def build_anthropic_kwargs(
   510→    model: str,
   511→    messages: List[Dict],
   512→    tools: Optional[List[Dict]],
   513→    max_tokens: Optional[int],
   514→    reasoning_config: Optional[Dict[str, Any]],
   515→    tool_choice: Optional[str] = None,
   516→) -> Dict[str, Any]:
   517→    """Build kwargs for anthropic.messages.create()."""
   518→    system, anthropic_messages = convert_messages_to_anthropic(messages)
   519→    anthropic_tools = convert_tools_to_anthropic(tools) if tools else []
   520→
   521→    model = normalize_model_name(model)
   522→    effective_max_tokens = max_tokens or 16384
   523→
   524→    kwargs: Dict[str, Any] = {
   525→        "model": model,
   526→        "messages": anthropic_messages,
   527→        "max_tokens": effective_max_tokens,
   528→    }
   529→
   530→    if system:
   531→        kwargs["system"] = system
   532→
   533→    if anthropic_tools:
   534→        kwargs["tools"] = anthropic_tools
   535→        # Map OpenAI tool_choice to Anthropic format
   536→        if tool_choice == "auto" or tool_choice is None:
   537→            kwargs["tool_choice"] = {"type": "auto"}
   538→        elif tool_choice == "required":
   539→            kwargs["tool_choice"] = {"type": "any"}
   540→        elif tool_choice == "none":
   541→            pass  # Don't send tool_choice — Anthropic will use tools if needed
   542→        elif isinstance(tool_choice, str):
   543→            # Specific tool name
   544→            kwargs["tool_choice"] = {"type": "tool", "name": tool_choice}
   545→
   546→    # Map reasoning_config to Anthropic's thinking parameter.
   547→    # Claude 4.6 models use adaptive thinking + output_config.effort.
   548→    # Older models use manual thinking with budget_tokens.
   549→    # Haiku models do NOT support extended thinking at all — skip entirely.
   550→    if reasoning_config and isinstance(reasoning_config, dict):
   551→        if reasoning_config.get("enabled") is not False and "haiku" not in model.lower():
   552→            effort = str(reasoning_config.get("effort", "medium")).lower()
   553→            budget = THINKING_BUDGET.get(effort, 8000)
   554→            if _supports_adaptive_thinking(model):
   555→                kwargs["thinking"] = {"type": "adaptive"}
   556→                kwargs["output_config"] = {
   557→                    "effort": ADAPTIVE_EFFORT_MAP.get(effort, "medium")
   558→                }
   559→            else:
   560→                kwargs["thinking"] = {"type": "enabled", "budget_tokens": budget}
   561→                # Anthropic requires temperature=1 when thinking is enabled on older models
   562→                kwargs["temperature"] = 1
   563→                kwargs["max_tokens"] = max(effective_max_tokens, budget + 4096)
   564→
   565→    return kwargs
   566→
   567→
   568→def normalize_anthropic_response(
   569→    response,
   570→) -> Tuple[SimpleNamespace, str]:
   571→    """Normalize Anthropic response to match the shape expected by AIAgent.
   572→
   573→    Returns (assistant_message, finish_reason) where assistant_message has
   574→    .content, .tool_calls, and .reasoning attributes.
   575→    """
   576→    text_parts = []
   577→    reasoning_parts = []
   578→    tool_calls = []
   579→
   580→    for block in response.content:
   581→        if block.type == "text":
   582→            text_parts.append(block.text)
   583→        elif block.type == "thinking":
   584→            reasoning_parts.append(block.thinking)
   585→        elif block.type == "tool_use":
   586→            tool_calls.append(
   587→                SimpleNamespace(
   588→                    id=block.id,
   589→                    type="function",
   590→                    function=SimpleNamespace(
   591→                        name=block.name,
   592→                        arguments=json.dumps(block.input),
   593→                    ),
   594→                )
   595→            )
   596→
   597→    # Map Anthropic stop_reason to OpenAI finish_reason
   598→    stop_reason_map = {
   599→        "end_turn": "stop",
   600→        "tool_use": "tool_calls",
   601→        "max_tokens": "length",
   602→        "stop_sequence": "stop",
   603→    }
   604→    finish_reason = stop_reason_map.get(response.stop_reason, "stop")
   605→
   606→    return (
   607→        SimpleNamespace(
   608→            content="\n".join(text_parts) if text_parts else None,
   609→            tool_calls=tool_calls or None,
   610→            reasoning="\n\n".join(reasoning_parts) if reasoning_parts else None,
   611→            reasoning_content=None,
   612→            reasoning_details=None,
   613→        ),
   614→        finish_reason,
   615→    )
   616→
```

257→# Tools whose execution is intercepted by the agent loop (run_agent.py)
   258→# because they need agent-level state (TodoStore, MemoryStore, etc.).
   259→# The registry still holds their schemas; dispatch just returns a stub error
   260→# so if something slips through, the LLM sees a sensible message.
   261→_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS = {"todo", "memory", "session_search", "delegate_task"}
   262→
   263→
   264→def handle_function_call(
   265→    function_name: str,
   266→    function_args: Dict[str, Any],
   267→    task_id: Optional[str] = None,
   268→    user_task: Optional[str] = None,
   269→    enabled_tools: Optional[List[str]] = None,
   270→) -> str:
   271→    """
   272→    Main function call dispatcher that routes calls to the tool registry.
   273→
   274→    Args:
   275→        function_name: Name of the function to call.
   276→        function_args: Arguments for the function.
   277→        task_id: Unique identifier for terminal/browser session isolation.
   278→        user_task: The user's original task (for browser_snapshot context).
   279→        enabled_tools: Tool names enabled for this session.  When provided,
   280→                       execute_code uses this list to determine which sandbox
   281→                       tools to generate.  Falls back to the process-global
   282→                       ``_last_resolved_tool_names`` for backward compat.
   283→
   284→    Returns:
   285→        Function result as a JSON string.
   286→    """
   287→    # Notify the read-loop tracker when a non-read/search tool runs,
   288→    # so the *consecutive* counter resets (reads after other work are fine).
   289→    _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS = {"read_file", "search_files"}
   290→    if function_name not in _READ_SEARCH_TOOLS:
   291→        try:
   292→            from tools.file_tools import notify_other_tool_call
   293→            notify_other_tool_call(task_id or "default")
   294→        except Exception:
   295→            pass  # file_tools may not be loaded yet
   296→
   297→    try:
   298→        if function_name in _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS:
   299→            return json.dumps({"error": f"{function_name} must be handled by the agent loop"})
   300→
   301→        if function_name == "execute_code":
   302→            # Prefer the caller-provided list so subagents can't overwrite
   303→            # the parent's tool set via the process-global.
   304→            sandbox_enabled = enabled_tools if enabled_tools is not None else _last_resolved_tool_names
   305→            return registry.dispatch(
   306→                function_name, function_args,
   307→                task_id=task_id,
   308→                enabled_tools=sandbox_enabled,
   309→            )
   310→
   311→        return registry.dispatch(
   312→            function_name, function_args,
   313→            task_id=task_id,
   314→            user_task=user_task,
   315→        )
   316→
   317→    except Exception as e:
   318→        error_msg = f"Error executing {function_name}: {str(e)}"
   319→        logger.error(error_msg)
   320→        return json.dumps({"error": error_msg}, ensure_ascii=False)
   321→
   322→
   323→# =============================================================================
   324→# Backward-compat wrapper functions
   325→# =============================================================================
   326→

  1253→        if cmd_lower.startswith("/skills inspect"):

Excellent! Now I have enough information to provide you with a comprehensive analysis. Let me compile my findings:

## Comprehensive Analysis: Model Catalog & Provider Routing System

I've thoroughly analyzed the hermes-agent codebase. Here's the end-to-end understanding of the model catalog and provider routing system:

---

### 1. MODEL CATALOGS (hermes_cli/models.py)

**Structure:**
- **OPENROUTER_MODELS**: Static list of OpenRouter model IDs with display descriptions (lines 17-31)
  - Example: `("anthropic/claude-opus-4.6", "recommended")`
  - These are the primary user-facing models shown in CLI menus
  
- **_PROVIDER_MODELS**: Dict mapping canonical provider IDs to their available model lists (lines 33-80)
  - Keys: "nous", "openai-codex", "zai", "kimi-coding", "minimax", "minimax-cn", "anthropic"
  - Enables fallback when live API isn't reachable
  
- **_PROVIDER_LABELS**: Human-readable provider names for display (lines 82-92)
- **_PROVIDER_ALIASES**: Maps user-friendly aliases to canonical IDs (lines 94-105)
  - E.g., "glm" → "zai", "claude" → "anthropic"
- **_KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES**: Set of all valid provider names + aliases for validation

**Key Functions:**
- `parse_model_input()` (line 166): Parses `provider:model` syntax, supporting inline provider switching
  - Only treats `:` as delimiter if left side is a recognized provider name
  - Returns `(provider, model)` tuple
  
- `curated_models_for_provider()` (line 193): Returns provider-specific model catalog
  - Tries live API first, falls back to static _PROVIDER_MODELS
  - OpenRouter always returns static list (no live endpoint)
  
- `validate_requested_model()` (line 328): Comprehensive model validation
  - Format checks: non-empty, no whitespace
  - Custom endpoints bypass validation (any model accepted)
  - Probes live API to confirm model exists
  - Accepts models even if not in public listing (handles plan-exclusive models)
  - Returns dict: `{accepted, persist, recognized, message}`
  
- `list_available_providers()` (line 129): **Checks which providers have valid credentials**
  - Calls `resolve_runtime_provider(requested=pid)` for each provider
  - Sets `authenticated: bool` on each provider dict
  - This is the mechanism to know which providers are available at runtime

---

### 2. AUXILIARY CLIENT PROVIDER RESOLUTION (agent/auxiliary_client.py)

This module handles lightweight API calls for auxiliary tasks (compression, web extraction, vision analysis) separate from the main agent chat flow.

**resolve_provider_client() (line 536)**: Central router for getting configured clients
- **Args:**
  - `provider`: One of "openrouter", "nous", "openai-codex"/"codex", "zai", "kimi-coding", "minimax", "minimax-cn", "custom", "auto"
  - `model`: Optional model override; uses provider's default if None
  - `async_mode`: Returns AsyncOpenAI instead of OpenAI
  - `raw_codex`: If True, returns raw OpenAI client (for Responses API access) instead of adapter
  
- **Auth determination:**
  1. Env vars checked in provider-specific order via PROVIDER_REGISTRY
  2. For OpenRouter: `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` preferred, fallback to `OPENAI_API_KEY`
  3. For custom endpoints: `OPENAI_BASE_URL` + `OPENAI_API_KEY`
  4. For API-key providers (Kimi, Z.AI, MiniMax): checks provider's designated env vars
  5. For Nous/Codex: OAuth tokens from ~/.hermes/auth.json

- **Auto-detection chain (line 495):**
  ```
  OpenRouter → Nous Portal → Custom Endpoint → Codex OAuth → API-key Providers → None
  ```

- **Special handling:**
  - **Codex (OpenAI via OAuth)**: Returns `CodexAuxiliaryClient` adapter that translates chat.completions API calls to Responses API format (lines 138-252)
  - **Kimi Code keys** (sk-kimi-): Auto-routes to api.kimi.com/coding/v1 instead of default endpoint
  - **Default auxiliary models** per provider (lines 49-55):
    - zai: "glm-4.5-flash"
    - kimi-coding: "kimi-k2-turbo-preview"
    - minimax: "MiniMax-M2.5-highspeed"
    - anthropic: "claude-haiku-4-5-20251001"

- **Provider-specific headers:**
  - OpenRouter: HTTP-Referer, X-OpenRouter-Title, X-OpenRouter-Categories
  - Kimi: User-Agent: "KimiCLI/1.0"

- **Client caching** (line 820): Caches by (provider, async_mode) to avoid repeated auth lookups

---

### 3. RUNTIME PROVIDER RESOLUTION (hermes_cli/runtime_provider.py)

This resolves the **main agent's provider and credentials** for chat execution (not auxiliary tasks).

**resolve_runtime_provider() (line 114)**: Main entry point
- **Args:**
  - `requested`: Explicit provider override (e.g., "nous", "openai-codex", "anthropic")
  - `explicit_api_key`: Force a specific API key
  - `explicit_base_url`: Force a specific base URL

- **Resolution order:**
  1. Checks `requested` parameter
  2. Falls back to env var `HERMES_INFERENCE_PROVIDER`
  3. Falls back to config file `model.provider`
  4. Defaults to "auto"

- **Provider-specific branches:**
  - **Nous Portal**: Returns OAuth credentials (access_token or agent_key) from ~/.hermes/auth.json
    - Extra fields: `expires_at`, `base_url` (from NOUS_INFERENCE_BASE_URL env or default)
  - **OpenAI Codex**: Returns OAuth token from hermes auth store
    - api_mode: "codex_responses"
  - **Anthropic**: Returns API key from env (ANTHROPIC_TOKEN, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, etc.)
    - api_mode: "anthropic_messages" (native Messages API, not chat.completions)
  - **API-key providers** (Z.AI, Kimi, MiniMax): Calls `resolve_api_key_provider_credentials()`
  - **OpenRouter** (fallback): Intelligent base_url selection:
    - When provider="auto": prefers config base_url if no explicit request
    - When provider="openrouter" explicitly: skips OPENAI_BASE_URL (prevents lockout to custom endpoints)
    - Falls back to OPENROUTER_BASE_URL constant

- **Return format:**
  ```python
  {
      "provider": str,
      "api_mode": "chat_completions" | "codex_responses" | "anthropic_messages",
      "base_url": str,
      "api_key": str,
      "source": "env" | "config" | "portal" | "hermes-auth-store",
      "requested_provider": str,
      # Provider-specific extras:
      "expires_at": (Nous),
      "last_refresh": (Codex),
  }
  ```

---

### 4. PROVIDER AVAILABILITY CHECKING

**How the system knows which providers have valid credentials:**

Method 1: **hermes_cli/models.py::list_available_providers()** (line 129)
```python
# Checks each provider's auth availability
for pid in _PROVIDER_ORDER:
    try:
        runtime = resolve_runtime_provider(requested=pid)
        has_creds = bool(runtime.get("api_key"))
    except Exception:
        has_creds = False
    result.append({...with "authenticated": has_creds...})
```

Method 2: **hermes_cli/auth.py::resolve_provider()** (line 503)
- Returns the best available provider based on configured credentials
- Used by CLI setup and gateway initialization
- Checks in priority order defined by _PROVIDER_ORDER

Method 3: **PROVIDER_REGISTRY** (hermes_cli/auth.py, line 95)
- Registry of all known providers with their auth types and env var names
- `ProviderConfig` contains:
  - `auth_type`: "oauth_device_code", "oauth_external", or "api_key"
  - `api_key_env_vars`: tuple of env vars to check (in priority order)
  - `base_url_env_var`: optional env var for endpoint override

---

### 5. FALLBACK MECHANISM (run_agent.py::_try_activate_fallback)

The main agent can switch to a fallback provider when the primary fails.

**Location:** Lines 2575-2658 in run_agent.py

**How it works:**
1. **Initialization** (lines 528-534):
   - Stores fallback config as `self._fallback_model = {"provider": "...", "model": "..."}`
   - Flag `self._fallback_activated = False` (one-shot activation)
   
2. **Activation triggers** (called from retry loop at lines 4290, 4707, 4728):
   - When primary model fails after max retries
   - When non-retryable client error detected (401, 403, 404, 422, invalid model, etc.)
   - When invalid API response (None choices, rate limit indicators)

3. **What gets swapped in-place** (lines 2615-2645):
   - `self.model`: Model slug
   - `self.provider`: Provider ID
   - `self.base_url`: API base URL
   - `self.api_mode`: API variant ("chat_completions", "codex_responses", "anthropic_messages")
   - `self.client`: OpenAI client instance
   - `self._client_kwargs`: Stored kwargs for client reconstruction
   - For Anthropic: `self._anthropic_client` and `self._anthropic_api_key`
   - `self._use_prompt_caching`: Re-evaluated for new provider/model

4. **Key gotchas & edge cases:**
   - **One-shot only**: Returns False if already activated (prevents cascading fallbacks)
   - **Raw Codex handling**: Uses `raw_codex=True` when calling `resolve_provider_client()` so the main loop can access responses.stream() directly
   - **Anthropic detection**: Swaps from OpenAI client to native Anthropic client for native API mode
   - **Prompt caching re-evaluation**: Checks if new model supports prompt caching (OpenRouter Claude or native Anthropic)
   - **Auth resolution**: Uses centralized `resolve_provider_client()` to avoid duplicated provider→key mappings
   - **Error recovery**: If fallback client construction fails, returns False silently (logs warning) and retries original provider

---

### 6. TOOLSETS (toolsets.py)

**Architecture:**
- Toolsets are composable groups of tools defined in the `TOOLSETS` dict
- Each toolset entry has: `description`, `tools` (direct), `includes` (other toolsets)

**Core tools pool** (_HERMES_CORE_TOOLS, lines 31-67):
- 67 total tools across: web, terminal, files, vision, browser, cronjob, skills, MoA, memory, etc.

**Toolset types:**

1. **Basic/Leaf toolsets** (direct tools only):
   - "web": web_search, web_extract
   - "search": web_search only
   - "vision": vision_analyze
   - "terminal": terminal, process
   - "skills": skills_list, skill_view, skill_manage
   - "browser": Browser automation + web_search
   - "code_execution": execute_code
   - "delegation": delegate_task
   - etc.

2. **Composite toolsets** (reference other toolsets):
   - "debugging": includes "web" + "file" + direct [terminal, process]
   - "safe": includes "web" + "vision" + "image_gen" (no terminal)

3. **Platform toolsets** (all share _HERMES_CORE_TOOLS):
   - "hermes-cli": Full CLI access
   - "hermes-telegram", "hermes-discord", "hermes-slack", etc.: Platform-specific (all get same tools)
   - "hermes-gateway": Union of all platform toolsets

**Composable resolution:**
- `resolve_toolset(name)`: Recursively resolves including toolsets to flat tool list
- Handles cycles (prints warning, returns [])
- Special aliases: "all" or "*" = all tools across all toolsets

**No "meta" or "agent" toolset exists** — all platforms use the same _HERMES_CORE_TOOLS pool. For a model-switching tool, you would either:
- Add it to _HERMES_CORE_TOOLS if it's universally appropriate
- Create a new composite toolset like "admin": `{"tools": ["model_switch"], "includes": ["hermes-cli"]}`
- Or just add "model_switch" directly to a specific platform's tools

---

## Summary Table

| Component | Location | Purpose | Key Methods |
|-----------|----------|---------|-------------|
| **Model Catalog** | hermes_cli/models.py | Static + live model lists per provider | `parse_model_input()`, `validate_requested_model()`, `list_available_providers()` |
| **Auxiliary Routing** | agent/auxiliary_client.py | Lightweight side tasks (compression, vision) | `resolve_provider_client()`, auto-detection chain |
| **Runtime Provider** | hermes_cli/runtime_provider.py | Main agent provider + credentials | `resolve_runtime_provider()` |
| **Auth Registry** | hermes_cli/auth.py | All known providers + env var mappings | `PROVIDER_REGISTRY`, `resolve_provider()`, `resolve_api_key_provider_credentials()` |
| **Fallback** | run_agent.py (lines 2575-2658) | Switch to backup provider on failure | `_try_activate_fallback()` |
| **Toolsets** | toolsets.py | Composable tool groups | `resolve_toolset()`, `TOOLSETS` dict |

---

## Critical Design Patterns

1. **Centralized provider routing**: Both auxiliary and main agent use the same PROVIDER_REGISTRY and auth resolution functions
2. **Config cascading**: Explicit arg → env var → config file → sensible default
3. **Lazy evaluation**: Providers only authenticated when actually needed (aux tasks, runtime setup)
4. **Codex special case**: OAuth token routes to Responses API, requires adapter for chat.completions compatibility
5. **Fallback is one-shot**: Prevents infinite cascade; designed for single retry with different backend
6. **Toolsets are composition-friendly**: Can nest other toolsets without code changes
