## Complete Flow Analysis: Tool Schemas from Registry to LLM API Call

I've thoroughly traced the tool flow through the codebase. Here's the complete picture:

---

### 1. **`get_tool_definitions()` in model_tools.py** (Lines 164-250)

**What it does:**
- **Tool discovery**: Imports all tool modules (lines 69-107), triggering their module-level `registry.register()` calls
- **Toolset-based filtering**: 
  - If `enabled_toolsets` provided: only includes tools from those toolsets (lines 185-189)
  - If `disabled_toolsets` provided: includes all tools except those toolsets (lines 201-216)
  - If neither: includes all tools from all toolsets (lines 220-223)
- **Registry query** (line 226): Calls `registry.get_definitions(tools_to_include, quiet=quiet_mode)` — this is the critical filtering step
- **Schema transformation** (lines 228-238): If `execute_code` is enabled, rebuilds its schema dynamically to only list sandbox tools that are actually available
- **Caches resolved tool names** (lines 247-248): Stores the final list globally for `execute_code` to reference

**Key transforms:**
- Passes `tools_to_include` (a `set`) to registry, which filters based on:
  - Tool existence in registry
  - **Availability checks** — each tool's `check_fn()` is called; tools are excluded if check fails (registry.py lines 95-104)
  
---

### 2. **Registry internals** (tools/registry.py)

**Registration** (lines 55-78):
Each tool module (e.g., `tools/todo_tool.py`, `tools/memory_tool.py`) calls:
```python
registry.register(
    name="todo",
    toolset="todo",
    schema=TODO_SCHEMA,  # OpenAI-format function schema
    handler=lambda args, **kw: todo_tool(..., store=kw.get("store")),
    check_fn=check_todo_requirements,
)
```

**Schema retrieval** (lines 84-106):
`registry.get_definitions(tool_names: Set[str])` returns OpenAI-format definitions:
```json
[
  {
    "type": "function",
    "function": {
      "name": "todo",
      "description": "...",
      "parameters": { "type": "object", "properties": {...} }
    }
  },
  ...
]
```

The only transformations applied here:
- Check function is called; tool is excluded if it returns False or raises
- Schema is wrapped in `{"type": "function", "function": schema}` format

---

### 3. **AIAgent Initialization** (run_agent.py, lines 537-549)

Tools are populated on the instance:
```python
self.tools = get_tool_definitions(
    enabled_toolsets=enabled_toolsets,
    disabled_toolsets=disabled_toolsets,
    quiet_mode=self.quiet_mode,
)
self.valid_tool_names = {tool["function"]["name"] for tool in self.tools}
```

This list is stored on the instance and used for every subsequent API call.

---

### 4. **System Prompt Construction** (run_agent.py, lines 1659-1800)

Tool-aware guidance is **conditionally injected** based on what tools are loaded:

```python
tool_guidance = []
if "memory" in self.valid_tool_names:
    tool_guidance.append(MEMORY_GUIDANCE)  # "You have persistent memory..."
if "session_search" in self.valid_tool_names:
    tool_guidance.append(SESSION_SEARCH_GUIDANCE)  # "When user references something..."
if "skill_manage" in self.valid_tool_names:
    tool_guidance.append(SKILLS_GUIDANCE)  # "After completing complex task..."
```

These guidance strings are **prepended to the system prompt** (lines 1699-1700), **NOT to the tools themselves**. No modifications happen to the tool schemas in the system prompt.

---

### 5. **API Call Construction** (run_agent.py, lines 2662-2773)

The `_build_api_kwargs()` method assembles the full API payload:

**For Anthropic Messages API** (lines 2664-2672):
```python
from agent.anthropic_adapter import build_anthropic_kwargs
return build_anthropic_kwargs(
    model=self.model,
    messages=api_messages,
    tools=self.tools,  # <-- Passed directly
    max_tokens=self.max_tokens,
    reasoning_config=self.reasoning_config,
)
```

**For OpenRouter/OpenAI-compatible** (lines 2728-2773):
```python
api_kwargs = {
    "model": self.model,
    "messages": api_messages,
    "tools": self.tools if self.tools else None,  # <-- Passed directly
    "timeout": 900.0,
}
```

The tools list is passed **as-is** (or `None` if empty) — no transformation occurs here.

---

### 6. **Provider-Specific Adaptation** (agent/anthropic_adapter.py)

**For Anthropic** (lines 357-369):
```python
def convert_tools_to_anthropic(tools: List[Dict]) -> List[Dict]:
    """Convert OpenAI tool definitions to Anthropic format."""
    result = []
    for t in tools:
        fn = t.get("function", {})
        result.append({
            "name": fn.get("name", ""),
            "description": fn.get("description", ""),
            "input_schema": fn.get("parameters", {...}),
        })
    return result
```

**For OpenAI/OpenRouter**: Tools are passed unchanged.

---

### 7. **Agent-Loop Tools: Interception Pattern**

**Critical design**: Agent-loop tools (`todo`, `memory`, `session_search`, `delegate_task`, `clarify`) are **NOT removed from the schema** — they stay in `self.tools` and are sent to the LLM.

**Interception flow:**

1. **Registry still holds schemas** (model_tools.py line 261):
   ```python
   _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS = {"todo", "memory", "session_search", "delegate_task", "clarify"}
   ```

2. **Dispatch stub** (model_tools.py lines 298-299): If the registry's `dispatch()` is called for these tools, it returns an error:
   ```python
   if function_name in _AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS:
       return json.dumps({"error": f"{function_name} must be handled by the agent loop"})
   ```
   This is a safety fallback in case a tool call somehow escapes interception.

3. **Agent-loop handles them directly** (run_agent.py, lines 3160-3214):
   ```python
   def _handle_agent_loop_tool(self, function_name: str, function_args: dict):
       if function_name == "todo":
           from tools.todo_tool import todo_tool as _todo_tool
           return _todo_tool(
               todos=function_args.get("todos"),
               merge=function_args.get("merge", False),
               store=self._todo_store,  # <-- Agent-level state
           )
       elif function_name == "session_search":
           ...
       elif function_name == "memory":
           ...
       elif function_name == "clarify":
           ...
       elif function_name == "delegate_task":
           ...
       else:
           return handle_function_call(...)  # Fallback for other tools
   ```

   These are called from `_execute_tool_calls_concurrent()` (lines 3450-3530) **before** any registry dispatch happens.

---

### Summary Table

| Stage | Component | Details |
|-------|-----------|---------|
| **Discovery** | `_discover_tools()` | Imports all tool modules; triggers `registry.register()` calls |
| **Filtering** | `get_tool_definitions()` | Applies toolset filtering + availability checks; returns OpenAI-format list |
| **Instance init** | `AIAgent.__init__()` | Stores tools on `self.tools`; caches `self.valid_tool_names` |
| **System prompt** | `_build_system_prompt()` | Injects behavioral guidance (memory, session_search, skills) based on loaded tools |
| **API assembly** | `_build_api_kwargs()` | Passes `self.tools` directly to provider adapter or OpenAI-compatible endpoint |
| **Provider xform** | `convert_tools_to_anthropic()` | Transforms OpenAI format → Anthropic format (for Anthropic only) |
| **Execution** | `_handle_agent_loop_tool()` | Agent loop intercepts todo/memory/session_search/delegate_task/clarify before registry dispatch; others go to `handle_function_call()` |

## User-Facing Experience Analysis: Model Changes and Agent Running

Based on my thorough analysis of the codebase, here's how users see model changes, agent status, and tool execution:

### 1. **Model Display During Initialization**

When the agent starts, the CLI displays the current model prominently:

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/run_agent.py` (lines 465, 513, 534)**

- For native Anthropic models: `🤖 AI Agent initialized with model: {model} (Anthropic native)`
- For other providers: `🤖 AI Agent initialized with model: {model}`
- If a fallback is configured: `🔄 Fallback model: {fallback_model} ({fallback_provider})`

The agent is initialized with `quiet_mode=True` in the CLI (line 1406 of `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py`), which means these startup messages are suppressed during normal CLI usage.

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 1397-1427)**

Instead, the CLI displays model info through two mechanisms:

### 2. **Welcome Banner with Model Display**

The welcome banner (`build_welcome_banner` function, line 786) shows:
- Model name (shortened for display): `[{_accent}]{model_short}[/]{ctx_str} [dim {_dim}]·[/] [dim {_dim}]Nous Research[/]`
- Context length: formatted alongside the model name
- Current working directory
- Session ID
- Available tools grouped by toolset

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 837-849)**

The banner pulls skin/theme colors and renders as a table with the Hermes caduceus ASCII art on the left.

### 3. **Status Bar Display**

The compact status bar (`_show_status` function, line 1852) displays:
- API connection indicator (green/red bullet)
- Model name (shortened)
- Tool count
- Enabled toolsets (if filtered)
- Provider name
- Auth source (if available)

**Example output:**
```
  ● [#FFBF00]claude-opus-4.6[/] [dim]·[/] [bold #4dd0e1]14 tools[/] [#CD7F32]toolsets: terminal, file[/][dim]provider: openrouter[/]
```

### 4. **Fallback Activation - User Experience**

When `_try_activate_fallback()` fires (run_agent.py, line 2575):

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/run_agent.py` (lines 2647-2654)**

The user **immediately sees** a message:
```
🔄 Primary model failed — switching to fallback: {fallback_model} via {fallback_provider}
```

This is **not silent** — it prints to the console with the `log_prefix` prepended. The fallback activation:
1. Swaps the client/model/provider in-place
2. Re-evaluates prompt caching capabilities
3. Logs the change via both `print()` and `logging.info()`

There is **no user confirmation needed** — the fallback happens automatically after max retries are exhausted on the primary model.

### 5. **Log Prefix and Display**

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/run_agent.py` (lines 314-315)**

The `log_prefix` is a string parameter that gets prepended to every print statement:
```python
self.log_prefix = f"{log_prefix} " if log_prefix else ""
```

This is used extensively in the conversation loop to show which agent or process is outputting (useful for parallel processing). Every print statement in the main loop includes `{self.log_prefix}` prefix.

In the CLI, when running single-query or interactive mode, `log_prefix` is typically empty, so no prefix appears.

### 6. **Tool Execution Display**

When the agent processes tool calls, users see:

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/run_agent.py` (lines 4149-4151, 4910, 4922)**

- API call initiation: `🔄 Making API call #{api_call_count}/{max_iterations}...`
- Request summary: `📊 Request size: {messages} messages, ~{tokens} tokens`
- Tool processing: `🔧 Processing {count} tool call(s)...`
- Tool repairs: `🔧 Auto-repaired tool name: '{old}' -> '{new}'`
- Tool result preview: Shows first N characters (controlled by `log_prefix_chars` parameter)

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/run_agent.py` (line 3272, 3424, 3614)**

Tool call args and results are truncated:
```python
args_preview = args_str[:self.log_prefix_chars] + "..." if len(args_str) > self.log_prefix_chars else args_str
response_preview = function_result[:self.log_prefix_chars] + "..." if len(function_result) > self.log_prefix_chars else function_result
```

Default: 100 characters for the main agent, 20 for parallelized calls.

### 7. **Conversation Loop Integration**

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 3597-3646)**

The conversation runs in a background thread:
1. User input is collected in the main TUI
2. `agent.run_conversation()` is called in a background thread
3. While running, the TUI shows a spinner and thinking text via `_on_thinking()` callback
4. The interrupt system monitors for new messages and can interrupt the agent

The TUI is invalidated (redrawn) via `_invalidate()` calls, which updates the spinner and status display while the agent runs.

### 8. **Thinking Display**

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 1241-1244)**

When the model is thinking (for models with reasoning), the callback updates:
```python
def _on_thinking(self, text: str) -> None:
    """Called by agent when thinking starts/stops. Updates TUI spinner."""
    self._spinner_text = text or ""
    self._invalidate()
```

The spinner text appears in the TUI while the agent thinks.

### 9. **Tool Progress Modes**

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 1029-1030, 3064-3080)**

Users can toggle tool progress visibility with the `/progress` command:
- `off` - No tool output
- `new` - Show new tool calls only
- `all` - Show all tool execution (default)
- `verbose` - Show detailed tool call args and results

This controls whether the extensive logging from the agent loop is shown to the user.

### 10. **Status Summary Display**

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/hermes-agent/cli.py` (lines 3750-3766)**

When users request session info, the CLI shows:
- Message count (broken down by user, assistant, tool calls)
- Session metadata
- Model currently in use
- Tools available

---

## Key Takeaways

1. **Model is never hidden** — displayed in the welcome banner and status bar at startup
2. **Fallback activation is visible** — users see `🔄 Primary model failed — switching to fallback` message immediately
3. **No user confirmation** — fallback happens automatically after exhausting retries
4. **Tool execution is verbose by default** — users see tool calls, results, and API call metrics
5. **log_prefix** allows parallel agents to tag their output for identification
6. **TUI invalidation** keeps the spinner and status updated in real-time while the agent runs in the background
7. **Tool progress can be toggled** for users who want less verbosity

> DEVELOPER

I need to design a "switch_model" tool that lets the Hermes agent change its own LLM model mid-conversation. Here's the full context from my codebase exploration:

## Architecture Context

### How tools work in Hermes:
1. Tool modules register schemas+handlers in `tools/registry.py` at import time
2. `model_tools.py:get_tool_definitions()` resolves toolsets, filters by availability, returns OpenAI-format schemas
3. `AIAgent.__init__()` stores `self.tools` and `self.valid_tool_names` 
4. Tools are passed to the LLM in every API call via `_build_api_kwargs()`
5. When LLM calls a tool, either:
   - **Agent-loop tools** (todo, memory, delegate_task, clarify, session_search) are intercepted in `run_agent.py:_execute_agent_loop_tool()` where they have access to `self` (the AIAgent instance)
   - **Regular tools** go through `model_tools.py:handle_function_call()` → `registry.dispatch()` with no agent access

### How models are configured:
- `AIAgent` has: `self.model`, `self.provider`, `self.base_url`, `self.api_mode`, `self.client` (OpenAI), `self._anthropic_client`
- Three API modes: "chat_completions" (OpenRouter/OpenAI-compat), "codex_responses" (OpenAI Codex), "anthropic_messages" (native Anthropic)
- `agent/auxiliary_client.py:resolve_provider_client(provider, model)` is the centralized client factory
- `hermes_cli/models.py` has model catalogs per provider (`_PROVIDER_MODELS`), validation (`validate_requested_model()`), and `list_available_providers()` which checks which providers have valid credentials

### Existing model-switching precedent:
- `_try_activate_fallback()` in run_agent.py (line 2575) already swaps model/client/provider mid-conversation
- It swaps: `self.model`, `self.provider`, `self.base_url`, `self.api_mode`, `self.client`, `self._client_kwargs`, `self._anthropic_client`, `self._anthropic_api_key`, `self._use_prompt_caching`
- Uses `resolve_provider_client()` for client construction
- Is one-shot (tracked by `_fallback_activated` flag)

### Tool registration pattern (example from clarify_tool.py):
```python
from tools.registry import registry
registry.register(
    name="clarify",
    toolset="clarify", 
    schema=CLARIFY_SCHEMA,
    handler=lambda args, **kw: clarify_tool(...),
    check_fn=check_clarify_requirements,
)
```

### Agent-loop interception pattern:
- `_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS` set in model_tools.py prevents registry dispatch
- `_execute_agent_loop_tool()` in run_agent.py handles them with `self` access
- The method is called from `_execute_tool_calls_concurrent()` (line 3450+) before registry dispatch

### Toolsets (toolsets.py):
- `_HERMES_CORE_TOOLS` is the master list of ~67 tools used by all platforms
- Toolsets are composable groups with `tools` and `includes` keys
- No existing "meta" or "agent" toolset

### System prompt:
- Tool-specific guidance is conditionally injected: `if "memory" in self.valid_tool_names: tool_guidance.append(MEMORY_GUIDANCE)`
- Built in `_build_system_prompt()` around line 1659

### User-facing display:
- Welcome banner shows model name
- Status bar shows model + provider
- Fallback prints: `🔄 Primary model failed — switching to fallback: {model} via {provider}`
- No user confirmation for fallback — it just happens

### Provider availability:
- `hermes_cli/models.py:list_available_providers()` checks which providers have valid credentials by calling `resolve_runtime_provider(requested=pid)` for each
- `hermes_cli/auth.py:PROVIDER_REGISTRY` has all known providers with auth types and env var names

## Design Requirements

The user wants:
1. The agent to know what models/providers are available to it
2. The agent to be able to switch its own model mid-conversation
3. A clean integration into the existing tool and user-facing flow

## Key Design Questions to Address

1. **How does the agent know what's available?** Should the tool schema include a static list? Should there be a separate "list_models" tool? Should the system prompt include available models?
2. **How does the switch actually execute?** Reuse `_try_activate_fallback()` pattern or create a new method?
3. **Should there be guardrails?** Max switches? Only authenticated providers? User confirmation?
4. **What toolset does this belong to?** New toolset? Add to core tools?
5. **How does the user see the switch?** Print message? Update status bar?
6. **Should the agent be able to switch back?** Store original model?
7. **One tool or two?** (list_models + switch_model) vs (one tool with an "action" param)

Please design a detailed, practical implementation plan that covers all these aspects. Prefer simplicity and reuse of existing infrastructure. Think about edge cases like: what if the provider isn't authenticated? What if the model doesn't exist? What if we're mid-tool-call?

```
1→"""Central registry for all hermes-agent tools.
     2→
     3→Each tool file calls ``registry.register()`` at module level to declare its
     4→schema, handler, toolset membership, and availability check.  ``model_tools.py``
     5→queries the registry instead of maintaining its own parallel data structures.
     6→
     7→Import chain (circular-import safe):
     8→    tools/registry.py  (no imports from model_tools or tool files)
     9→           ^
    10→    tools/*.py  (import from tools.registry at module level)
    11→           ^
    12→    model_tools.py  (imports tools.registry + all tool modules)
    13→           ^

    36→        self.schema = schema
    37→        self.handler = handler
    38→        self.check_fn = check_fn

    43→
    44→class ToolRegistry:
    45→    """Singleton registry that collects tool schemas + handlers from tool files."""
    46→
    47→    def __init__(self):
    48→        self._tools: Dict[str, ToolEntry] = {}
    49→        self._toolset_checks: Dict[str, Callable] = {}
    50→
    51→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    52→    # Registration
    53→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    54→
    55→    def register(
    56→        self,
    57→        name: str,
    58→        toolset: str,
    59→        schema: dict,
    60→        handler: Callable,
    61→        check_fn: Callable = None,
    62→        requires_env: list = None,
    63→        is_async: bool = False,
    64→        description: str = "",
    65→    ):

    67→        self._tools[name] = ToolEntry(
    68→            name=name,
    69→            toolset=toolset,
    70→            schema=schema,
    71→            handler=handler,
    72→            check_fn=check_fn,
    73→            requires_env=requires_env or [],
    74→            is_async=is_async,
    75→            description=description or schema.get("description", ""),
    76→        )
    77→        if check_fn and toolset not in self._toolset_checks:
    78→            self._toolset_checks[toolset] = check_fn
    79→
    80→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    81→    # Schema retrieval
    82→    # ------------------------------------------------------------------
    83→
    84→    def get_definitions(self, tool_names: Set[str], quiet: bool = False) -> List[dict]:
    85→        """Return OpenAI-format tool schemas for the requested tool names.
    86→
    87→        Only tools whose ``check_fn()`` returns True (or have no check_fn)
    88→        are included.
    89→        """
    90→        result = []
    91→        for name in sorted(tool_names):
    92→            entry = self._tools.get(name)
    93→            if not entry:
    94→                continue
    95→            if entry.check_fn:
    96→                try:
    97→                    if not entry.check_fn():
    98→                        if not quiet:
    99→                            logger.debug("Tool %s unavailable (check failed)", name)
   100→                        continue
   101→                except Exception:
   102→                    if not quiet:

   104→                    continue
   105→            result.append({"type": "function", "function": entry.schema})

   109→    # Dispatch

   112→    def dispatch(self, name: str, args: dict, **kwargs) -> str:
   113→        """Execute a tool handler by name.
   114→
   115→        * Async handlers are bridged automatically via ``_run_async()``.

   126→            return entry.handler(args, **kwargs)

   129→            return json.dumps({"error": f"Tool execution failed: {type(e).__name__}: {e}"})

   148→    def is_toolset_available(self, toolset: str) -> bool:
   149→        """Check if a toolset's requirements are met.
   150→
   151→        Returns False (rather than crashing) when the check function raises
   152→        an unexpected exception (e.g. network error, missing import, bad config).
   153→        """
   154→        check = self._toolset_checks.get(toolset)
   155→        if not check:
   156→            return True
   157→        try:
   158→            return bool(check())
   159→        except Exception:
   160→            logger.debug("Toolset %s check raised; marking unavailable", toolset)
   161→            return False
   162→
   163→    def check_toolset_requirements(self) -> Dict[str, bool]:
   164→        """Return ``{toolset: available_bool}`` for every toolset."""
   165→        toolsets = set(e.toolset for e in self._tools.values())
   166→        return {ts: self.is_toolset_available(ts) for ts in sorted(toolsets)}
   167→
   168→    def get_available_toolsets(self) -> Dict[str, dict]:
   169→        """Return toolset metadata for UI display."""
   170→        toolsets: Dict[str, dict] = {}
   171→        for entry in self._tools.values():
   172→            ts = entry.toolset
   173→            if ts not in toolsets:
   174→                toolsets[ts] = {

   176→                    "tools": [],

   180→            toolsets[ts]["tools"].append(entry.name)
   181→            if entry.requires_env:
```

```
   166→def parse_model_input(raw: str, current_provider: str) -> tuple[str, str]:
   167→    """Parse ``/model`` input into ``(provider, model)``.
   168→
   169→    Supports ``provider:model`` syntax to switch providers at runtime::
   170→
   171→        openrouter:anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5  →  ("openrouter", "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5")
   172→        nous:hermes-3                           →  ("nous", "hermes-3")
   173→        anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5             →  (current_provider, "anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.5")
   174→        gpt-5.4                                 →  (current_provider, "gpt-5.4")
   175→
   176→    The colon is only treated as a provider delimiter if the left side is a
   177→    recognized provider name or alias.  This avoids misinterpreting model names
   178→    that happen to contain colons (e.g. ``anthropic/claude-3.5-sonnet:beta``).
   179→
   180→    Returns ``(provider, model)`` where *provider* is either the explicit
   181→    provider from the input or *current_provider* if none was specified.
   182→    """
   183→    stripped = raw.strip()
   184→    colon = stripped.find(":")
   185→    if colon > 0:
   186→        provider_part = stripped[:colon].strip().lower()
   187→        model_part = stripped[colon + 1:].strip()
   188→        if provider_part and model_part and provider_part in _KNOWN_PROVIDER_NAMES:
   189→            return (normalize_provider(provider_part), model_part)
   190→    return (current_provider, stripped)
   191→

   193→def curated_models_for_provider(provider: Optional[str]) -> list[tuple[str, str]]:
   194→    """Return ``(model_id, description)`` tuples for a provider's model list.
   195→
   196→    Tries to fetch the live model list from the provider's API first,
   197→    falling back to the static ``_PROVIDER_MODELS`` catalog if the API
   198→    is unreachable.
   199→    """
   200→    normalized = normalize_provider(provider)
   201→    if normalized == "openrouter":
   202→        return list(OPENROUTER_MODELS)
   203→
   204→    # Try live API first (Codex, Nous, etc. all support /models)
   205→    live = provider_model_ids(normalized)
   206→    if live:
   207→        return [(m, "") for m in live]
   208→
   209→    # Fallback to static catalog
   210→    models = _PROVIDER_MODELS.get(normalized, [])
   211→    return [(m, "") for m in models]
   212→
   213→
   214→def normalize_provider(provider: Optional[str]) -> str:
   215→    """Normalize provider aliases to Hermes' canonical provider ids.
   216→
   217→    Note: ``"auto"`` passes through unchanged — use
   218→    ``hermes_cli.auth.resolve_provider()`` to resolve it to a concrete
   219→    provider based on credentials and environment.
   220→    """
   221→    normalized = (provider or "openrouter").strip().lower()
   222→    return _PROVIDER_ALIASES.get(normalized, normalized)
   223→
   224→
   225→def provider_model_ids(provider: Optional[str]) -> list[str]:
   226→    """Return the best known model catalog for a provider.
   227→
   228→    Tries live API endpoints for providers that support them (Codex, Nous),
   229→    falling back to static lists.
   230→    """
   231→    normalized = normalize_provider(provider)
   232→    if normalized == "openrouter":
   233→        return model_ids()
   234→    if normalized == "openai-codex":
   235→        from hermes_cli.codex_models import get_codex_model_ids
   236→
   237→        return get_codex_model_ids()
   238→    if normalized == "nous":
   239→        # Try live Nous Portal /models endpoint
   240→        try:
   241→            from hermes_cli.auth import fetch_nous_models, resolve_nous_runtime_credentials
   242→            creds = resolve_nous_runtime_credentials()
   243→            if creds:
   244→                live = fetch_nous_models(creds.get("api_key", ""), creds.get("base_url", ""))
   245→                if live:
   246→                    return live
   247→        except Exception:
   248→            pass
   249→    if normalized == "anthropic":
   250→        live = _fetch_anthropic_models()
   251→        if live:
   252→            return live
   253→    return list(_PROVIDER_MODELS.get(normalized, []))
   254→
   255→
   256→def _fetch_anthropic_models(timeout: float = 5.0) -> Optional[list[str]]:
   257→    """Fetch available models from the Anthropic /v1/models endpoint.
   258→
   259→    Uses resolve_anthropic_token() to find credentials (env vars or
   260→    Claude Code auto-discovery).  Returns sorted model IDs or None.
   261→    """
   262→    try:
   263→        from agent.anthropic_adapter import resolve_anthropic_token, _is_oauth_token
   264→    except ImportError:
   265→        return None
   266→
   267→    [REDACTED]()
   268→    if not token:
   269→        return None
   270→
   271→    headers: dict[str, str] = {"anthropic-version": "2023-06-01"}
   272→    if _is_oauth_token(token):
   273→        headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {token}"
   274→        from agent.anthropic_adapter import _COMMON_BETAS, _OAUTH_ONLY_BETAS
   275→        headers["anthropic-beta"] = ",".join(_COMMON_BETAS + _OAUTH_ONLY_BETAS)
   276→    else:
   277→        headers["x-api-key"] = token
   278→
   279→    req = urllib.request.Request(
   280→        "https://api.anthropic.com/v1/models",
   281→        headers=headers,
   282→    )
   283→    try:
   284→        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as resp:
   285→            data = json.loads(resp.read().decode())
   286→            models = [m["id"] for m in data.get("data", []) if m.get("id")]
   287→            # Sort: latest/largest first (opus > sonnet > haiku, higher version first)
   288→            return sorted(models, key=lambda m: (
   289→                "opus" not in m,      # opus first
   290→                "sonnet" not in m,    # then sonnet
   291→                "haiku" not in m,     # then haiku
   292→                m,                    # alphabetical within tier
   293→            ))
   294→    except Exception as e:
   295→        import logging
   296→        logging.getLogger(__name__).debug("Failed to fetch Anthropic models: %s", e)
   297→        return None
   298→
   299→
   300→def fetch_api_models(
   301→    api_key: Optional[str],
   302→    base_url: Optional[str],
   303→    timeout: float = 5.0,
   304→) -> Optional[list[str]]:
   305→    """Fetch the list of available model IDs from the provider's ``/models`` endpoint.
   306→
   307→    Returns a list of model ID strings, or ``None`` if the endpoint could not
   308→    be reached (network error, timeout, auth failure, etc.).
   309→    """
   310→    if not base_url:
   311→        return None
   312→
   313→    url = base_url.rstrip("/") + "/models"
   314→    headers: dict[str, str] = {}
   315→    if api_key:
   316→        headers["Authorization"] = f"Bearer {api_key}"
   317→
   318→    req = urllib.request.Request(url, headers=headers)
   319→    try:
   320→        with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout) as resp:
   321→            data = json.loads(resp.read().decode())
   322→            # Standard OpenAI format: {"data": [{"id": "model-name", ...}, ...]}
   323→            return [m.get("id", "") for m in data.get("data", [])]
   324→    except Exception:
   325→        return None
   326→
   327→
   328→def validate_requested_model(
   329→    model_name: str,
   330→    provider: Optional[str],
   331→    *,
   332→    api_key: Optional[str] = None,
   333→    base_url: Optional[str] = None,
   334→) -> dict[str, Any]:
   335→    """
   336→    Validate a ``/model`` value for the active provider.
   337→
   338→    Performs format checks first, then probes the live API to confirm
   339→    the model actually exists.
   340→
   341→    Returns a dict with:
   342→      - accepted: whether the CLI should switch to the requested model now
   343→      - persist: whether it is safe to save to config
   344→      - recognized: whether it matched a known provider catalog
   345→      - message: optional warning / guidance for the user
   346→    """
   347→    requested = (model_name or "").strip()
   348→    normalized = normalize_provider(provider)
   349→    if normalized == "openrouter" and base_url and "openrouter.ai" not in base_url:
   350→        normalized = "custom"
   351→
   352→    if not requested:
   353→        return {
   354→            "accepted": False,
   355→            "persist": False,
   356→            "recognized": False,
   357→            "message": "Model name cannot be empty.",
   358→        }
   359→
   360→    if any(ch.isspace() for ch in requested):
   361→        return {
   362→            "accepted": False,
   363→            "persist": False,
   364→            "recognized": False,
   365→            "message": "Model names cannot contain spaces.",
   366→        }
   367→
   368→    # Custom endpoints can serve any model — skip validation
   369→    if normalized == "custom":
   370→        return {
   371→            "accepted": True,
   372→            "persist": True,
   373→            "recognized": False,
   374→            "message": None,
   375→        }
   376→
   377→    # Probe the live API to check if the model actually exists
   378→    api_models = fetch_api_models(api_key, base_url)
   379→
   380→    if api_models is not None:
   381→        if requested in set(api_models):
   382→            # API confirmed the model exists
   383→            return {
   384→                "accepted": True,
   385→                "persist": True,
   386→                "recognized": True,
   387→                "message": None,
   388→            }
   389→        else:
   390→            # API responded but model is not listed.  Accept anyway —
   391→            # the user may have access to models not shown in the public
   392→            # listing (e.g. Z.AI Pro/Max plans can use glm-5 on coding
   393→            # endpoints even though it's not in /models).  Warn but allow.
   394→            suggestions = get_close_matches(requested, api_models, n=3, cutoff=0.5)
   395→            suggestion_text = ""
   396→            if suggestions:
   397→                suggestion_text = "\n  Similar models: " + ", ".join(f"`{s}`" for s in suggestions)
   398→
   399→            return {
   400→                "accepted": True,
   401→                "persist": True,
   402→                "recognized": False,
   403→                "message": (
   404→                    f"Note: `{requested}` was not found in this provider's model listing. "
   405→                    f"It may still work if your plan supports it."
   406→                    f"{suggestion_text}"
   407→                ),
   408→            }
   409→
   410→    # api_models is None — couldn't reach API.  Accept and persist,
   411→    # but warn so typos don't silently break things.
   412→    provider_label = _PROVIDER_LABELS.get(normalized, normalized)
   413→    return {
   414→        "accepted": True,
   415→        "persist": True,
   416→        "recognized": False,
   417→        "message": (
   418→            f"Could not reach the {provider_label} API to validate `{requested}`. "
   419→            f"If the service isn't down, this model may not be valid."
   420→        ),
   421→    }
   422→
```

```
  2581→        already activated or not configured.

  2584→        auth resolution and client construction — no duplicated provider→key
  2585→        mappings.

  2618→            self.model = fb_model
```

```
3216→    def _execute_tool_calls_concurrent(self, assistant_message, messages: list, effective_task_id: str, api_call_count: int = 0) -> None:
  3217→        """Execute multiple tool calls concurrently using a thread pool.
  3218→
  3219→        Results are collected in the original tool-call order and appended to
  3220→        messages so the API sees them in the expected sequence.
  3221→        """
  3222→        tool_calls = assistant_message.tool_calls
  3223→        num_tools = len(tool_calls)
  3224→
  3225→        # ── Pre-flight: interrupt check ──────────────────────────────────
  3226→        if self._interrupt_requested:
  3227→            print(f"{self.log_prefix}⚡ Interrupt: skipping {num_tools} tool call(s)")
  3228→            for tc in tool_calls:
  3229→                messages.append({
  3230→                    "role": "tool",
  3231→                    "content": f"[Tool execution cancelled — {tc.function.name} was skipped due to user interrupt]",
  3232→                    "tool_call_id": tc.id,
  3233→                })
  3234→            return
  3235→
  3236→        # ── Parse args + pre-execution bookkeeping ───────────────────────
  3237→        parsed_calls = []  # list of (tool_call, function_name, function_args)
  3238→        for tool_call in tool_calls:
  3239→            function_name = tool_call.function.name
  3240→
  3241→            # Reset nudge counters
  3242→            if function_name == "memory":
  3243→                self._turns_since_memory = 0
  3244→            elif function_name == "skill_manage":
  3245→                self._iters_since_skill = 0
  3246→
  3247→            try:
  3248→                function_args = json.loads(tool_call.function.arguments)
  3249→            except json.JSONDecodeError:
  3250→                function_args = {}
  3251→            if not isinstance(function_args, dict):
  3252→                function_args = {}
  3253→
  3254→            # Checkpoint for file-mutating tools
  3255→            if function_name in ("write_file", "patch") and self._checkpoint_mgr.enabled:
  3256→                try:
  3257→                    file_path = function_args.get("path", "")
  3258→                    if file_path:
  3259→                        work_dir = self._checkpoint_mgr.get_working_dir_for_path(file_path)
  3260→                        self._checkpoint_mgr.ensure_checkpoint(work_dir, f"before {function_name}")
  3261→                except Exception:
  3262→                    pass
  3263→
  3264→            parsed_calls.append((tool_call, function_name, function_args))
  3265→
  3266→        # ── Logging / callbacks ──────────────────────────────────────────
  3267→        tool_names_str = ", ".join(name for _, name, _ in parsed_calls)
  3268→        if not self.quiet_mode:
  3269→            print(f"  ⚡ Concurrent: {num_tools} tool calls — {tool_names_str}")
  3270→            for i, (tc, name, args) in enumerate(parsed_calls, 1):
  3271→                args_str = json.dumps(args, ensure_ascii=False)
  3272→                args_preview = args_str[:self.log_prefix_chars] + "..." if len(args_str) > self.log_prefix_chars else args_str
  3273→                print(f"  📞 Tool {i}: {name}({list(args.keys())}) - {args_preview}")
  3274→
  3275→        for _, name, args in parsed_calls:
  3276→            if self.tool_progress_callback:
  3277→                try:
  3278→                    preview = _build_tool_preview(name, args)
  3279→                    self.tool_progress_callback(name, preview, args)
  3280→                except Exception as cb_err:
  3281→                    logging.debug(f"Tool progress callback error: {cb_err}")
  3282→
  3283→        # ── Concurrent execution ─────────────────────────────────────────
  3284→        # Each slot holds (function_name, function_args, function_result, duration, error_flag)
  3285→        results = [None] * num_tools
  3286→
  3287→        def _run_tool(index, tool_call, function_name, function_args):
  3288→            """Worker function executed in a thread."""
  3289→            start = time.time()
  3290→            try:
  3291→                result = self._invoke_tool(function_name, function_args, effective_task_id)
  3292→            except Exception as tool_error:
  3293→                result = f"Error executing tool '{function_name}': {tool_error}"
  3294→                logger.error("_invoke_tool raised for %s: %s", function_name, tool_error, exc_info=True)
  3295→            duration = time.time() - start
  3296→            is_error, _ = _detect_tool_failure(function_name, result)
  3297→            results[index] = (function_name, function_args, result, duration, is_error)
  3298→
  3299→        # Start spinner for CLI mode
  3300→        spinner = None
  3301→        if self.quiet_mode:
  3302→            face = random.choice(KawaiiSpinner.KAWAII_WAITING)
  3303→            spinner = KawaiiSpinner(f"{face} ⚡ running {num_tools} tools concurrently", spinner_type='dots')
  3304→            spinner.start()
  3305→
  3306→        try:
  3307→            max_workers = min(num_tools, _MAX_TOOL_WORKERS)
  3308→            with concurrent.futures.ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max_workers) as executor:
  3309→                futures = []
  3310→                for i, (tc, name, args) in enumerate(parsed_calls):
  3311→                    f = executor.submit(_run_tool, i, tc, name, args)
  3312→                    futures.append(f)
  3313→
  3314→                # Wait for all to complete (exceptions are captured inside _run_tool)
  3315→                concurrent.futures.wait(futures)
  3316→        finally:
  3317→            if spinner:
  3318→                # Build a summary message for the spinner stop
  3319→                completed = sum(1 for r in results if r is not None)
  3320→                total_dur = sum(r[3] for r in results if r is not None)
  3321→                spinner.stop(f"⚡ {completed}/{num_tools} tools completed in {total_dur:.1f}s total")
  3322→
  3323→        # ── Post-execution: display per-tool results ─────────────────────
  3324→        for i, (tc, name, args) in enumerate(parsed_calls):
  3325→            r = results[i]
  3326→            if r is None:
  3327→                # Shouldn't happen, but safety fallback
  3328→                function_result = f"Error executing tool '{name}': thread did not return a result"
  3329→                tool_duration = 0.0
  3330→            else:
  3331→                function_name, function_args, function_result, tool_duration, is_error = r
  3332→
  3333→                if is_error:
  3334→                    result_preview = function_result[:200] if len(function_result) > 200 else function_result
  3335→                    logger.warning("Tool %s returned error (%.2fs): %s", function_name, tool_duration, result_preview)
```

```
3154→    def _invoke_tool(self, function_name: str, function_args: dict, effective_task_id: str) -> str:
  3155→        """Invoke a single tool and return the result string. No display logic.
  3156→
  3157→        Handles both agent-level tools (todo, memory, etc.) and registry-dispatched
  3158→        tools. Used by the concurrent execution path; the sequential path retains
  3159→        its own inline invocation for backward-compatible display handling.
  3160→        """
  3161→        if function_name == "todo":
  3162→            from tools.todo_tool import todo_tool as _todo_tool
  3163→            return _todo_tool(
  3164→                todos=function_args.get("todos"),
  3165→                merge=function_args.get("merge", False),
  3166→                store=self._todo_store,
  3167→            )
  3168→        elif function_name == "session_search":
  3169→            if not self._session_db:
  3170→                return json.dumps({"success": False, "error": "Session database not available."})
  3171→            from tools.session_search_tool import session_search as _session_search
  3172→            return _session_search(
  3173→                query=function_args.get("query", ""),
  3174→                role_filter=function_args.get("role_filter"),
  3175→                limit=function_args.get("limit", 3),
  3176→                db=self._session_db,
  3177→                current_session_id=self.session_id,
  3178→            )
  3179→        elif function_name == "memory":
  3180→            target = function_args.get("target", "memory")
  3181→            from tools.memory_tool import memory_tool as _memory_tool
  3182→            result = _memory_tool(
  3183→                action=function_args.get("action"),
  3184→                target=target,
  3185→                content=function_args.get("content"),
  3186→                old_text=function_args.get("old_text"),
  3187→                store=self._memory_store,
  3188→            )
  3189→            # Also send user observations to Honcho when active
  3190→            if self._honcho and target == "user" and function_args.get("action") == "add":
  3191→                self._honcho_save_user_observation(function_args.get("content", ""))
  3192→            return result
  3193→        elif function_name == "clarify":
  3194→            from tools.clarify_tool import clarify_tool as _clarify_tool
  3195→            return _clarify_tool(
  3196→                question=function_args.get("question", ""),
  3197→                choices=function_args.get("choices"),
  3198→                callback=self.clarify_callback,
  3199→            )
  3200→        elif function_name == "delegate_task":
  3201→            from tools.delegate_tool import delegate_task as _delegate_task
  3202→            return _delegate_task(
  3203→                goal=function_args.get("goal"),
  3204→                context=function_args.get("context"),
  3205→                toolsets=function_args.get("toolsets"),
  3206→                tasks=function_args.get("tasks"),
  3207→                max_iterations=function_args.get("max_iterations"),
  3208→                parent_agent=self,
  3209→            )
  3210→        else:
  3211→            return handle_function_call(
  3212→                function_name, function_args, effective_task_id,
  3213→                enabled_tools=list(self.valid_tool_names) if self.valid_tool_names else None,
```

```
1→"""Shared auxiliary OpenAI client for cheap/fast side tasks.
     2→
     3→Provides a single resolution chain so every consumer (context compression,
     4→session search, web extraction, vision analysis, browser vision) picks up
     5→the best available backend without duplicating fallback logic.
     6→
     7→Resolution order for text tasks (auto mode):
     8→  1. OpenRouter  (OPENROUTER_API_KEY)
     9→  2. Nous Portal (~/.hermes/auth.json active provider)
    10→  3. Custom endpoint (OPENAI_BASE_URL + OPENAI_API_KEY)
    11→  4. Codex OAuth (Responses API via chatgpt.com with gpt-5.3-codex,
    12→     wrapped to look like a chat.completions client)
    13→  5. Direct API-key providers (z.ai/GLM, Kimi/Moonshot, MiniMax, MiniMax-CN)
    14→     — checked via PROVIDER_REGISTRY entries with auth_type='api_key'
    15→  6. None
    16→
    17→Resolution order for vision/multimodal tasks (auto mode):
    18→  1. OpenRouter
    19→  2. Nous Portal
    20→  3. Codex OAuth (gpt-5.3-codex supports vision via Responses API)
    21→  4. Custom endpoint (for local vision models: Qwen-VL, LLaVA, Pixtral, etc.)
    22→  5. None  (API-key providers like z.ai/Kimi/MiniMax are skipped —
    23→     they may not support multimodal)
    24→
    25→Per-task provider overrides (e.g. AUXILIARY_VISION_PROVIDER,
    26→CONTEXT_COMPRESSION_PROVIDER) can force a specific provider for each task:
    27→"openrouter", "nous", "codex", or "main" (= steps 3-5).
    28→Default "auto" follows the chains above.
    29→
    30→Per-task model overrides (e.g. AUXILIARY_VISION_MODEL,
    31→AUXILIARY_WEB_EXTRACT_MODEL) let callers use a different model slug
    32→than the provider's default.
    33→"""
    34→
    35→import json
    36→import logging
    37→import os
    38→from pathlib import Path
    39→from types import SimpleNamespace
    40→from typing import Any, Dict, List, Optional, Tuple
    41→
    42→from openai import OpenAI
    43→
    44→from hermes_constants import OPENROUTER_BASE_URL
    45→
    46→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    47→
    48→# Default auxiliary models for direct API-key providers (cheap/fast for side tasks)
    49→_API_KEY_PROVIDER_AUX_MODELS: Dict[str, str] = {
    50→    "zai": "glm-4.5-flash",
    51→    "kimi-coding": "kimi-k2-turbo-preview",
    52→    "minimax": "MiniMax-M2.5-highspeed",
    53→    "minimax-cn": "MiniMax-M2.5-highspeed",
    54→    "anthropic": "claude-haiku-4-5-20251001",
    55→}
    56→
    57→# OpenRouter app attribution headers
    58→_OR_HEADERS = {
    59→    "HTTP-Referer": "https://hermes-agent.nousresearch.com",
    60→    "X-OpenRouter-Title": "Hermes Agent",
    61→    "X-OpenRouter-Categories": "productivity,cli-agent",
    62→}
    63→
    64→# Nous Portal extra_body for product attribution.
    65→# Callers should pass this as extra_body in chat.completions.create()
    66→# when the auxiliary client is backed by Nous Portal.
    67→NOUS_EXTRA_BODY = {"tags": ["product=hermes-agent"]}
    68→
    69→# Set at resolve time — True if the auxiliary client points to Nous Portal
    70→auxiliary_is_nous: bool = False
    71→
    72→# Default auxiliary models per provider
    73→_OPENROUTER_MODEL = "google/gemini-3-flash-preview"
    74→_NOUS_MODEL = "gemini-3-flash"
    75→_NOUS_DEFAULT_BASE_URL = "https://inference-api.nousresearch.com/v1"
    76→_AUTH_JSON_PATH = Path.home() / ".hermes" / "auth.json"
    77→
    78→# Codex fallback: uses the Responses API (the only endpoint the Codex
    79→# OAuth token can access) with a fast model for auxiliary tasks.
    80→_CODEX_AUX_MODEL = "gpt-5.3-codex"
```

```
536→def resolve_provider_client(
   537→    provider: str,
   538→    model: str = None,
   539→    async_mode: bool = False,
   540→    raw_codex: bool = False,
   541→) -> Tuple[Optional[Any], Optional[str]]:
   542→    """Central router: given a provider name and optional model, return a
   543→    configured client with the correct auth, base URL, and API format.
   544→
   545→    The returned client always exposes ``.chat.completions.create()`` — for
   546→    Codex/Responses API providers, an adapter handles the translation
   547→    transparently.
   548→
   549→    Args:
   550→        provider: Provider identifier.  One of:
   551→            "openrouter", "nous", "openai-codex" (or "codex"),
   552→            "zai", "kimi-coding", "minimax", "minimax-cn",
   553→            "custom" (OPENAI_BASE_URL + OPENAI_API_KEY),
   554→            "auto" (full auto-detection chain).
   555→        model: Model slug override.  If None, uses the provider's default
   556→               auxiliary model.
   557→        async_mode: If True, return an async-compatible client.
   558→        raw_codex: If True, return a raw OpenAI client for Codex providers
   559→            instead of wrapping in CodexAuxiliaryClient.  Use this when
   560→            the caller needs direct access to responses.stream() (e.g.,
   561→            the main agent loop).
   562→
   563→    Returns:
   564→        (client, resolved_model) or (None, None) if auth is unavailable.
   565→    """
   566→    # Normalise aliases
   567→    provider = (provider or "auto").strip().lower()
   568→    if provider == "codex":
   569→        provider = "openai-codex"
   570→    if provider == "main":
   571→        provider = "custom"
   572→
   573→    # ── Auto: try all providers in priority order ────────────────────
   574→    if provider == "auto":
   575→        client, resolved = _resolve_auto()
   576→        if client is None:
   577→            return None, None
   578→        final_model = model or resolved
   579→        return (_to_async_client(client, final_model) if async_mode
   580→                else (client, final_model))
   581→
   582→    # ── OpenRouter ───────────────────────────────────────────────────
   583→    if provider == "openrouter":
   584→        client, default = _try_openrouter()
   585→        if client is None:
   586→            logger.warning("resolve_provider_client: openrouter requested "
   587→                           "but OPENROUTER_API_KEY not set")
   588→            return None, None
   589→        final_model = model or default
   590→        return (_to_async_client(client, final_model) if async_mode
   591→                else (client, final_model))
   592→
   593→    # ── Nous Portal (OAuth) ──────────────────────────────────────────
   594→    if provider == "nous":
   595→        client, default = _try_nous()
   596→        if client is None:
   597→            logger.warning("resolve_provider_client: nous requested "
   598→                           "but Nous Portal not configured (run: hermes login)")
   599→            return None, None
   600→        final_model = model or default
   601→        return (_to_async_client(client, final_model) if async_mode
   602→                else (client, final_model))
   603→
   604→    # ── OpenAI Codex (OAuth → Responses API) ─────────────────────────
   605→    if provider == "openai-codex":
   606→        if raw_codex:
   607→            # Return the raw OpenAI client for callers that need direct
   608→            # access to responses.stream() (e.g., the main agent loop).
   609→            [REDACTED]()
   610→            if not codex_token:
   611→                logger.warning("resolve_provider_client: openai-codex requested "
   612→                               "but no Codex OAuth token found (run: hermes model)")
   613→                return None, None
   614→            final_model = model or _CODEX_AUX_MODEL
   615→            raw_client = OpenAI(api_key=codex_token, base_url=_CODEX_AUX_BASE_URL)
```

```
    13→
    14→import json
    15→from typing import Dict, Any, List, Optional, Callable
    16→
    17→
    18→# Maximum number of predefined choices the agent can offer.
    19→# A 5th "Other (type your answer)" option is always appended by the UI.
    20→MAX_CHOICES = 4
    21→
    22→
    23→def clarify_tool(
    24→    question: str,
    25→    choices: Optional[List[str]] = None,
    26→    callback: Optional[Callable] = None,

    28→    """
    29→    Ask the user a question, optionally with multiple-choice options.
    30→
    31→    Args:
    32→        question: The question text to present.
    33→        choices:  Up to 4 predefined answer choices. When omitted the
    34→                  question is purely open-ended.
    35→        callback: Platform-provided function that handles the actual UI
    36→                  interaction. Signature: callback(question, choices) -> str.
    37→                  Injected by the agent runner (cli.py / gateway).
    38→
    39→    Returns:
    40→        JSON string with the user's response.
    41→    """
    42→    if not question or not question.strip():
    43→        return json.dumps({"error": "Question text is required."}, ensure_ascii=False)
    44→
    45→    question = question.strip()
    46→
    47→    # Validate and trim choices
    48→    if choices is not None:
    49→        if not isinstance(choices, list):
    50→            return json.dumps({"error": "choices must be a list of strings."}, ensure_ascii=False)
    51→        choices = [str(c).strip() for c in choices if str(c).strip()]
    52→        if len(choices) > MAX_CHOICES:
    53→            choices = choices[:MAX_CHOICES]
    54→        if not choices:
    55→            choices = None  # empty list → open-ended
    56→
    57→    if callback is None:
    58→        return json.dumps(
    59→            {"error": "Clarify tool is not available in this execution context."},
    60→            ensure_ascii=False,
    61→        )
    62→
    63→    try:
    64→        user_response = callback(question, choices)
    65→    except Exception as exc:
    66→        return json.dumps(
    67→            {"error": f"Failed to get user input: {exc}"},
    68→            ensure_ascii=False,
    69→        )
    70→
    71→    return json.dumps({

    73→        "choices_offered": choices,
    74→        "user_response": str(user_response).strip(),
```

---

## Implementation Plan: `switch_model` Tool

### Design Decisions

**One tool, not two.** A single `switch_model` tool with an `action` parameter (`"list"` or `"switch"`) is the right choice. Two tools would clutter the tool list for a feature that is used infrequently. The `list` action lets the agent discover what is available before switching, while keeping the schema compact.

**Agent-loop tool, not a registry-dispatched tool.** The switch requires mutating `self.model`, `self.client`, `self.provider`, and several other AIAgent fields. This is the same pattern as `todo`, `memory`, `clarify`, and `delegate_task` -- all of which are handled in `_invoke_tool()` because they need `self` access. The tool schema still lives in the registry (for schema discovery and availability checking), but dispatch is intercepted before registry dispatch.

**Reuse `_try_activate_fallback` logic via a new `_switch_model` method.** The fallback method is one-shot and has extra semantics (fallback flag, error message about primary model failing). A new `_switch_model(provider, model)` method on AIAgent will extract the core client-swapping logic from `_try_activate_fallback` into a shared helper, or simply duplicate the ~30 lines of client construction -- the latter is simpler and avoids changing fallback behavior.

**No user confirmation required.** The agent already switches models without confirmation in the fallback path. The switch_model tool is the agent's own decision, just like picking which tool to call. The user sees a clear status message.

**No max-switch limit.** The agent's iteration limit already constrains runaway behavior. Artificial switch limits would be arbitrary and frustrating.

**Store original model for switching back.** A `self._original_model` dict (captured once at init) lets the agent return to its starting configuration.

### File-by-File Plan

#### 1. `tools/switch_model_tool.py` (NEW FILE)

This file defines:

- `SWITCH_MODEL_SCHEMA` -- OpenAI function-calling schema with:
  - `action`: enum `["list", "switch"]`, required
  - `provider`: string, required only when action is `"switch"`
  - `model`: string, required only when action is `"switch"`
  - `reason`: string, optional -- why the agent is switching (logged for observability)

- `switch_model_list()` function -- calls `list_available_providers()` from `hermes_cli/models.py` and `curated_models_for_provider()` to build a JSON response listing all authenticated providers and their models. Returns JSON string.

- `switch_model_switch(provider, model, agent)` function -- the actual switch logic. This:
  1. Validates provider is known (via `normalize_provider`)
  2. Validates model is non-empty
  3. Calls `agent._switch_model(provider, model)` (the new AIAgent method)
  4. Returns JSON with success/error status, old model, new model

- `check_switch_model_requirements()` -- always returns `True` (no external deps)

- Registry registration with `toolset="switch_model"`, following the pattern from `clarify_tool.py`. The handler is a lambda stub since this is an agent-loop tool.

Schema sketch:
```python
SWITCH_MODEL_SCHEMA = {
    "name": "switch_model",
    "description": (
        "Switch the LLM model being used for this conversation, or list "
        "available models and providers.\n\n"
        "Actions:\n"
        "- 'list': Return all available providers and their models\n"
        "- 'switch': Switch to a specific provider:model combination\n\n"
        "Use this when a different model would be better suited for the "
        "current task (e.g., switching to a coding-focused model for code "
        "generation, or a larger model for complex reasoning)."
    ),
    "parameters": {
        "type": "object",
        "properties": {
            "action": {
                "type": "string",
                "enum": ["list", "switch"],
                "description": "Whether to list available models or switch to one."
            },
            "provider": {
                "type": "string",
                "description": "Provider ID to switch to (e.g., 'openrouter', 'anthropic', 'nous'). Required for 'switch' action."
            },
            "model": {
                "type": "string",
                "description": "Model ID to switch to. Required for 'switch' action."
            },
            "reason": {
                "type": "string",
                "description": "Brief explanation of why you're switching models."
            }
        },
        "required": ["action"]
    }
}
```

#### 2. `run_agent.py` -- Two changes

**a. Add `_switch_model()` method** (near `_try_activate_fallback`, around line 2660):

This method takes `(provider: str, model: str)` and performs the swap. It mirrors lines 2596-2655 of `_try_activate_fallback` but without the one-shot flag or fallback semantics:

1. Calls `resolve_provider_client(provider, model, raw_codex=True)`
2. If client is None, returns error dict
3. Determines `api_mode` from provider
4. Saves current model/provider to `self._previous_model` (for switch-back)
5. Swaps: `self.model`, `self.provider`, `self.base_url`, `self.api_mode`, `self.client`, `self._client_kwargs`, `self._anthropic_client`, `self._anthropic_api_key`, `self._use_prompt_caching`
6. Prints status message: `"🔄 Model switched: {old} → {new} via {provider}"`
7. Calls `self.tool_progress_callback` if set (updates status bar)
8. Returns success dict

Also store `self._original_model = {"provider": self.provider, "model": self.model, "base_url": self.base_url}` in `__init__` so the agent can reference it.

**b. Add `switch_model` case to `_invoke_tool()`** (around line 3200, after `delegate_task`):

```python
elif function_name == "switch_model":
    action = function_args.get("action", "list")
    if action == "list":
        from tools.switch_model_tool import switch_model_list
        return switch_model_list()
    elif action == "switch":
        from tools.switch_model_tool import switch_model_switch
        return switch_model_switch(
            provider=function_args.get("provider", ""),
            model=function_args.get("model", ""),
            reason=function_args.get("reason", ""),
            agent=self,
        )
    else:
        return json.dumps({"error": f"Unknown action: {action}"})
```

Add `SWITCH_MODEL_GUIDANCE` alongside the existing guidance strings:

```python
    "You can switch your own LLM model mid-conversation using switch_model. "
    "Use 'list' first to see available providers and models, then 'switch' "
```

The `{provider}:{model}` placeholders would be filled in at prompt-build time from `self._original_model`.

#### 6. `run_agent.py` -- System prompt injection (line ~1697):

```python

    from agent.prompt_builder import SWITCH_MODEL_GUIDANCE
    guidance = SWITCH_MODEL_GUIDANCE.format(
        provider=self._original_model["provider"],
```

### Edge Cases

1. **Provider not authenticated**: `resolve_provider_client` returns `(None, None)`. The tool returns a clear error: `"Provider 'X' is not configured. Run 'hermes setup' or set the appropriate API key."` The `list` action already marks providers as authenticated/unauthenticated so the agent can avoid this.

2. **Model doesn't exist**: The client will be created successfully (OpenAI client construction doesn't validate models). The error surfaces on the next API call. This is acceptable -- the agent will see the API error and can switch back. We could add a pre-check via `validate_requested_model()` but that adds latency for marginal benefit.

3. **Mid-tool-call switch**: Not possible. `switch_model` is itself a tool call; the switch takes effect after it returns. The next API call uses the new model. No concurrent execution concern because the switch happens in the tool execution flow, which is sequential for agent-loop tools.

4. **Switching back**: The agent sees `_original_model` info in the system prompt guidance and in the `list` action response (which can include a `current_model` field). It can call `switch_model(action="switch", provider=original_provider, model=original_model)`.

5. **Concurrent tool execution**: `switch_model` mutates agent state, so it must not run concurrently with other tools. The existing `_invoke_tool` runs in a thread pool, but agent-loop tools like `clarify` and `delegate_task` also mutate state and already run in this same path without issues because the mutations are atomic enough (Python's GIL protects the attribute assignments). For extra safety, we could check if `function_name == "switch_model"` in the concurrent execution path and force sequential execution for that batch, but this is likely unnecessary given the GIL.

6. **Tools change after switch**: The tool definitions passed to the LLM are rebuilt on every API call via `_build_api_kwargs()`. Since `self.tools` is set once in `__init__`, the same tools remain available. This is correct -- tool availability is a Hermes concern, not a model concern.

Create `tests/test_switch_model_tool.py`:

- Test `switch_model_switch()` with valid provider/model (mock `resolve_provider_client`)
- Test `switch_model_switch()` with unauthenticated provider returns error
- Test `switch_model_switch()` with empty model returns error
- Test the tool is in `_AGENT_LOOP_TOOLS`
- Test `_invoke_tool` dispatches `switch_model` correctly (mock agent)
